(5) Paid plan column in the limits doc (Pro/Business) corresponds to the Workers platform pricing (from $5/mo min) — Pages alone has no standalone paid subscription; '100 custom domains/project' on Free is per-project, multiplied across up to 100 projects.
  - *Source:* https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/platform/limits/;https://developers.cloudflare.com/pages/;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/pricing/
- **Cloudflare for Startups (startup credit program)** (high) — Official program page read today: up to $350,000 in credits for early-stage companies, tiered: Tier 3 = $10,000 credits (bootstrapped/self-funded, under $1M raised, no minimum funding, benefits: community/Discord/docs); Tier 2 = $100,000 (under $5M raised, funded by an affiliated partner; account manager case-dependent + expert sessions); Tier 1 = $350,000 ($5M+ raised, funded by an affiliated partner; account manager, priority support, bi-weekly Solutions Architect office hours). Credits cover usage-based services incl. Pages: Workers, Workers for Platforms, Durable Objects, Workflows, Workers AI, KV, D1, Queues, Vectorize, R2 (capped $10,000), Pages, Images and Stream, Cache Reserve, Argo Smart Routing. Product caps: Workers AI $2,500 (T3)/$10,000 (T2)/$50,000 (T1); R2 $10,000; AI Gateway temporarily NOT covered. Included at no cost in all tiers: 3 enterprise domains, unlimited Business and Pro plans, DDoS/bot protection, WAF with 1,000 custom rules, SSL, CDN+DNS, LB (2 origins), Rate Limiting (1 rule), 100% SLA. Excluded from credits: registrar fees, Enterprise add-ons, Magic Transit, Enterprise Spectrum, Dedicated IPs, China Network.
  - *Eligibility:* ALL must be met: for-profit business; incorporated/founded within the last 10 years; funded up to Series B (within last 12 months); actively building a technology product; publicly accessible live website; public presence on LinkedIn, X or GitHub; first-time applicants only; business email matching your domain (no personal email). Explicitly excluded: educational institutions, personal blogs, consultancies, service-based agencies, MSPs, resellers. No .edu (students use the free plan instead). No geographic restriction stated (China Network excluded and needs a dedicated contract).
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the landing page linked from cloudflare.com/startups (form at /lp/startups): fill out the application describing product and team; review typically within 48 hours; activation confirmation by email; questions to startups@cloudflare.com. A valid payment method must be uploaded on file as part of the program.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 1 year or until fully consumed, whichever comes first; Cloudflare states it cannot approve extensions. After expiration, the credit card on file is billed for usage unless a prepaid contract is signed.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for usage-based services only — the free Pages plan is $0 to begin with, so the program only pays for your overage/paid-plan consumption; 'no cost' bundle (3 enterprise domains, WAF, SLA) is the real structural win. CREDIT CARD / payment method IS required (unlike the free plan). Funding caps matter: Tier 1 needs an affiliated-partner round, not just any $5M+. The older regional sub-pages (en-gb/en-ca /forstartups/) still show the pre-2026 four-tier $5k/$25k/$100k/$250k structure — the main cloudflare.com/startups page is authoritative.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/startups/
- **Student / education offer** (medium) — No student-specific free offer exists for Pages or the Cloudflare developer platform. The official education page (read today) lists no academic license, no student credits, and no .edu-based benefit; the only quantified education program is 'Project Cybersafe Schools': free cloud email security and DNS filtering (CIPA-compliant) for small US K-12 public school districts — a security offering, not hosting, and not available to individual students/educators. Cloudflare's startup program explicitly excludes educational institutions. Individual students are served by the universal $0 Pages Free plan (no card, no application), which is the same as the general public's free tier; free developer courses/community exist but carry no hosting entitlement.
  - *Eligibility:* Students: no dedicated eligibility — they use the general Free plan (any account, no card). Project Cybersafe Schools: small US public K-12 districts. No .edu requirement anywhere; .edu email grants no special Cloudflare benefit.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim for students beyond the standard free plan. For K-12 districts: contact Cloudflare education team via the contact form on cloudflare.com/education.
  - *Expiry:* N/A — absence of a student program; free plan is permanent.
  - *Gotchas:* Search budget in this session was exhausted, so I could not exhaustively rule out ad-hoc student partnerships (e.g., accelerator/course-specific credit drops like the historical Workers Launchpad, which is partner-gated, not student-gated). Confidence is medium: I read the official education page today but student programs, if any, could live under partner/launchpad names I didn't fetch.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/education/
- **Cloudflare Free Plan (CDN + DDoS + DNS + basic WAF)** (high) — Perpetual $0/month free tier. Includes global CDN, always-on unlimited DDoS mitigation (no configuration), unlimited DNS zones/queries, TLS certificates, and basic WAF + Bot Fight Mode on the free tier. Runs on 100+ data centers across 330+ cities. Bundles the Workers Free compute tier and per-service free allowances (R2 10 GB, KV 1 GB, D1 5 GB, Durable Objects, Hyperdrive).
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone (students, startups, individuals, all regions). No .edu email, no age/funding cap. A credit card is NOT required: a Free account is created with just email + password.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at dash.cloudflare.com/sign-up with email + password (no CC). 2) Add your domain via CNAME or point name servers to Cloudflare. 3) CDN + DDoS protection auto-activate for free.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — active for the life of the account.
  - *Gotchas:* No CC needed to start, but adding a payment method unlocks paid products and any overage/egress beyond the free allowances is billed. Free plan has per-feature caps (e.g. Pages 500 builds/mo and 100 custom domains; 512 MB CDN cache).
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/
- **Cloudflare Workers Free Plan (Compute)** (high) — $0. 100,000 Worker invocations/day (resets 00:00 UTC), 10 ms CPU per request, up to 100 Workers, 64 MiB memory, 50 external + 1,000 Cloudflare-service subrequests per invocation. Free allowances also include KV 1 GB, R2 10 GB, D1 5 GB, Durable Objects, Hyperdrive 100k queries/day.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone on a Free Cloudflare account.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free account, then deploy a Worker with `npx wrangler deploy`. No credit card needed.
  - *Expiry:* Daily limits reset each day; plan is perpetual.
  - *Gotchas:* The task's 'known so far' figure of ~1M requests/day is OUTDATED — the official current free limit is 100,000/day. Hitting the cap returns Error 1027 unless you upgrade to Workers Paid ($5/mo min).
  - *Source:* https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/limits/
- **Cloudflare Observability / Monitoring / Uptime free tier** (high) — Shared Observability free allowance of 0.5 GB/day ingestion + 7-day retention, pooling Traces, Workers Logs, Real User Monitoring (RUM), AI Gateway logs, R2 access logs, and Issues. Workers Logs free tier = 200,000 events/day (3-day retention). Cloudflare Radar and RUM are available on the Free plan for monitoring/uptime-style visibility.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone on the Free plan; no CC or .edu required.
  - *How to claim:* In the dashboard, enable Analytics & Logs / Real User Monitoring; free to ingest up to 0.5 GB/day.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; daily ingestion resets at 00:00 UTC.
  - *Gotchas:* When the 0.5 GB/day cap is hit, ingestion stops until the reset. Scheduled change: from Dec 1, 2026, Workers Logs moves to Cloudflare Observability pricing — plan retention/limits may change after that date.
  - *Source:* https://developers.cloudflare.com/observability/pricing/
- **Cloudflare Startup Program (up to $350,000 in credits)** (high) — 3 tiers (2026): Tier 3 = $10k (bootstrapped/self-funded, under $1M raised, no minimum funding); Tier 2 = $100k (under $5M raised, funded by an affiliated partner); Tier 1 = $350k ($5M+ raised, affiliated partner). Credits apply to usage-based services (Workers, KV, D1, Queues, Vectorize, R2 up to $10k, Workers AI up to $50k, Images, Stream, Argo, Cache Reserve). Included at NO COST on every tier: 3 enterprise domains, unlimited Business/Pro plans, DDoS + bot protection, WAF with 1,000 custom rules, CDN + DNS, load balancing, 100% SLA.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit startup founded within the last 10 years, funded up to Series B within the last 12 months, actively building a tech product, with a public verifiable website, active on LinkedIn/X/GitHub, first-time applicant, and a business-domain email. Since Sep 2025, non-profit/civil-society/public-interest orgs are also eligible (up to $250k).
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloudflare.com/startups (form describing the product + team). You must create a Cloudflare account AND add a valid payment method on file (used to bill overages). Review takes up to ~48 hours; approval arrives by email.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid for 1 year or until fully consumed, whichever is first. No extensions; upgrading within a cohort does not reset the 12-month window.
  - *Gotchas:* Unlike the plain Free plan, the Startup program REQUIRES a payment method on file. Product-specific caps: R2 up to $10k; Workers AI $2.5k (Tier 3)/$10k (Tier 2)/$50k (Tier 1); AI Gateway temporarily NOT covered. 'Funded up to Series B within last 12 months' means recently-fundraised startups only.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/startups/
- **Cloudflare Academy — free courses & certification (education license)** (medium) — Self-paced, free online training curriculum plus free certification exams (e.g. Cloudflare Certified Associate). No .edu email, no student status, and no funding cap required — open to anyone. Educators can also run the material in classrooms; instructors can be supported through Cloudflare's instructor program.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone (students, educators, self-learners, all regions).
  - *How to claim:* Go to Cloudflare Academy, create a free account, and start any course for $0.
  - *Expiry:* Courses/certs do not expire; content updates over time.
  - *Gotchas:* Could not re-fetch the Academy page today (DNS resolution failure for academy.cloudflare.com from this environment), so the exact current course catalog/URL was not re-verified. The free/open/self-paced model and free-certification positioning are consistent with Cloudflare's learning/community pages but were not re-read on the live page.
  - *Source:* https://academy.cloudflare.com/
- **Cloudflare Ambassador & Community programs (student leaders)** (high) — The Cloudflare Community Ambassador program explicitly targets 'student leaders' (alongside community organizers and technical creators): run on-campus clubs, meetups, workshops, and content. Benefits include Cloudflare credits, marketing assets, swag, and access to Cloudflare experts; cohorts last 1 year with the option to reapply. A separate Community Engineer track provides discretionary grants for meaningful open-source work. Neither is a paid role.
  - *Eligibility:* Open application; non-technical enthusiasts welcome; student club leaders, community organizers, and open-source contributors.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through cloudflare.com/community under the Ambassador (or Community Engineer) track.
  - *Expiry:* Cohort-based: 1-year Ambassador cohorts, reapply to continue; Community Engineer has no fixed term.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits/grants are discretionary (awards, not a flat entitlement) and cohort-based, not a guaranteed free tier like the Free plan.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/community/
- **Cloudflare Free (Network & CDN) plan — always-free** (high) — $0/month website/CDN plan: CDN, unmetered DDoS protection, Universal SSL, WAF with Free Managed Ruleset, DNS, SSO, Role-based Account Control; 70 WAF rules + 10K bulk redirects; bot mitigation limited to 'Simple bots'; community forums support only. Paid tiers for comparison: Pro $20/mo annual ($25 monthly), Business $200/mo annual ($250 monthly), Enterprise custom. Page targets 'personal or hobby projects'.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone worldwide; no .edu email, no student status, no credit card/payment method required for the Free plan (sign up at dash.cloudflare.com/sign-up, 'Start building for free' — payment method only needed later to enable paid products/services).
  - *How to claim:* Create a free Cloudflare account (no payment method), add your domain as a zone, set nameservers to Cloudflare. Instant, perpetual, renews monthly at $0. The plan page (network-cdn) does not state a hard zone/site cap or bandwidth cap; Free has historically been unlimited-bandwidth with all sites/zones included.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent; no expiry. Free-plan feature sets can shrink/change with plan revisions.
  - *Gotchas:* Only 'Simple bots' mitigation and 70 rules on Free; WAF max payload and bot analytics are paid-plan features. Paid add-ons cost extra (e.g. Log Explorer $1/GB after 10 GB).
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/network-cdn/
- **Cloudflare Developer Free plan — Workers, R2, D1, KV, Pages, AI (perpetual, not a trial)** (high) — Perpetual free allowances (monthly unless noted): Workers 100,000 requests/day + 10 ms CPU/request (128 MB memory; 100 Workers; 5 cron triggers; 50 external subrequests/request); R2 10 GB-month Standard storage + 1M Class A + 10M Class B ops/month; D1 5 GB total storage + 5M rows read/day; Workers KV 1 GB storage + 100K reads/day; Pages 500 builds/month, 100 custom domains/project, 20K files, 20-min build timeout; Workers AI 10,000 neurons/day; Observability free: 0.5 GB/day ingestion, 7-day retention; Workflows 3,000 steps/day + 1 GB-month storage. No charge for egress/bandwidth on Workers and R2. Workers Paid plan is $5/mo minimum (includes 10M requests + 30M CPU ms).
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone; no .edu/student requirement, no funding/company status, and credit card NOT required to create a Free account — docs explicitly frame adding a payment method as a post-signup step to 'enable additional products and services'.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up free at dash.cloudflare.com/sign-up; free allowances are included automatically on any new account — no application, no key, no card. Exceeding Workers quotas returns error 1027 (fail-open/closed), not a bill, on the Free plan; overages on R2/D1/KV etc. do bill, which requires a payment method on the account.
  - *Expiry:* Permanent free tier; daily limits reset at midnight UTC, monthly allowances reset each billing month. 2026 additions started charging for some add-ons (see sunset_notes).
  - *Gotchas:* R2 free tier covers Standard storage only (Infrequent Access is never free). New metered add-ons (R2 SQL, R2 Data Catalog billed 2026-08-03; AI Search billing from 2026-11-01 with 5M ingestion tokens, 10 GB-month, 1K semantic + 1K full-text queries/month included) can produce invoices on an otherwise-free account — monitor 'Billable Usage' and set spend caps. Workers AI Gateway is 'temporarily not covered' by startup credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/
- **Cloudflare Startup Program — annual credits ($10K / $100K / $350K tiers)** (high) — Cloudflare's startup program (cloudflare.com/startup, apply at /lp/startups) offers credits valid 1 year or until consumed (extensions not granted). Tiers recently raised: Tier 3 $10,000/yr (was $5,000), 'no minimum funding required'; Tier 2 $100,000/yr + account manager; Tier 1 $350,000/yr (was $250,000) + Office Hours + priority support. Exclusions/caps: R2 capped at $10K credit; Workers AI capped at $2.5K/$10K/$50K by tier; AI Gateway temporarily not covered; domain registrar fees and premium network services (Magic Transit, Enterprise Spectrum, Dedicated IPs, China Network) excluded. Review typically ~48 hours, confirmation by email.
  - *Eligibility:* For-profit company only; incorporated no more than 10 years ago; funded up to Series B (funding within last 12 months, except Tier 3 needs no minimum funding); business email on the company domain — personal emails are rejected, so .edu/student emails are NOT eligible; must have a public website and public LinkedIn/X/GitHub presence; first-time applicant only; no region limit is stated on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the form at https://www.cloudflare.com/lp/startups with company/business-domain email; if accepted you receive the tier's annual credit to offset Cloudflare spend. The startup program is for companies, so students/universities/sole .edu users effectively cannot claim it.
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 12 months from grant or until consumed, whichever first; no extensions. Active members near old caps may email startups@cloudflare.com for case-by-case tier upgrade (does not reset the 12-month window).
  - *Gotchas:* Business-entity + company-domain email rules make it unusable for individual students, classes, or nonprofit/university projects; 'funding within last 12 months' + up-to-Series-B cap means late-stage and >10-year-old companies are out; entry tier is now $10K not the $5K referenced in older sources.
  - *Source:* https://www.cloudflare.com/startup/
- **Education / .edu programs — no dedicated student or university program found** (medium) — No .edu-only or student-exclusive Cloudflare offer was found today: no Cloudflare education license, no student credits, no university pricing page (cloudflare.com/academy and /lp/startups-education equivalents 404; academy.cloudflare.com did not resolve in this session; Cloudflare docs contain no 'academy/education' content). Cloudflare Academy (free courses/certifications for IT/security training, open to everyone — not .edu-gated) has existed in recent years, but its current status is unconfirmed from today's fetches. The only 'free' education-adjacent value is the general free developer plan plus free Radar data dashboards.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — negative finding: no .edu/student-email program exists to claim; free platform is available to students exactly as to anyone else (no card required).
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim. Students get the standard Free developer plan (Workers 100K req/day, R2 10 GB, D1 5 GB, KV 1 GB, Pages 500 builds/month) with no special student terms; universities would have to use the startup program only if they fit its for-profit company rules, which they typically do not.
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* If Cloudflare Academy has moved hosts (dead subdomain in this environment), re-verify before citing its free courses/certifications; treat any .edu-only claim as unsubstantiated.
  - *Source:* https://academy.cloudflare.com/(DNSdidnotresolve,2026-10-22)
- **2025–26 change:** Changes in 2025–2026: (1) The Cloudflare for Startups program was restructured from the April 2025 four-tier layout ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k, per blog post 'Startup Program update') to the current three-tier layout ($10k/$100k/$350k) — Tier 3 raised $5k→$10k and top tier $250k→$350k; regional pages (e.g. cloudflare.com/en-in/forstartups) still display the outdated $250k/4-tier structure, so use the US .com page as authoritative. (2) Sept 22, 2025 (Birthday Week 2025): Students program launched (12 months free Workers Paid for US .edu 18+) and the program was extended to non-profits/civil-society orgs (up to $250k credits). (3) Sept 2025: 'Come build with us' hubs — program participants get office access in SF/Austin/London/Lisbon from Jan 2026. (4) Sept 28, 2026: 'The Cold Start' competition announced — 5 early-stage companies pitch at Cloudflare Connect; grand prize $500,000 in credits (competition, not a standing program). (5) Workers AI pricing page (last updated Oct 20, 2026) moved to per-model granular unit-based pricing display while backend billing remains Neuron-based; 8 frontier models are now gated behind a paid billing method. Sunset of 2022–2024 programs: the original $1.25B/$2B-pledge Workers Launchpad funding program (2022) evolved into the current cohort Launchpad; no free-offer sunsets this cycle — all five offers above remain active as of 2026-10-22. Note: one follow-up web search (non-profit program page URL) could not be executed because the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted; non-profit and Launchpad findings lean on blog/press summaries and cached page content, hence medium confidence.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025–2026 program changes: (1) The Startup Program was restructured in 2025 from the April 2025 4-tier structure ($5,000 / $25,000 / $100,000 / $250,000, 'founded within last 5 years') to the current 3 tiers ($10k / $100k / $350k, founded within last 10 years) — entry tier doubled, top tier raised $250k->$350k, and the $25k tier was dropped. Members on the old caps can request a case-by-case upgrade that does not reset their 12-month expiry. (2) Sept 22, 2025: program expanded to non-profit, civil-society, and public-interest organizations (up to $250k credits; first application window ran until Dec 1, 2025). (3) Sept 22, 2025: Cloudflare for Students launched (12-month free paid developer features, US .edu only) — an education license/credit that did not exist before 2025. (4) 'The Cold Start' ($500k credit prize) closed with its Oct 19, 2026 Connect event; applications ended Oct 2, 2026. (5) Stale pages: some localized pages (e.g., cloudflare.com/en-in/forstartups/) still show the old '5 years / $5k-$250k' criteria — treat as outdated; the global /startups/ page is authoritative. (6) Community note: a global Cloudflare outage on Nov 18, 2025 (Workers KV, Access, Turnstile, dashboard, core CDN) is a recurring single-vendor risk with free tiers. The pre-launch claim 'WAF (1,000 rules) on all [startup] tiers' is confirmed — but the regular Free plan only gets 5 WAF custom rules; 1,000 is the Enterprise-plan limit that the startup program includes at no cost.
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22, Cloudflare has NO distinct free offers targeted at students or startups: (1) No startup program — the official partners page (cloudflare.com/partners, fetched 2026-10-22) lists only PowerUP channel program, technology alliances, global SIs, service providers, and the Cloudflare@OCI alliance; no startup track, no free credits, no age/funding-capped program. (2) No trial credits — paid Workers plans simply start at $5/mo with usage-based overage; no free-trial-of-paid credits are offered. (3) No education license/.edu discount for infrastructure — cloudflare.com/plans (fetched 2026-10-22) shows usage-based Free/Paid tiers only and mentions no education, student, or nonprofit discount; no .edu email is required for any tier; the free tiers are universally available to any account. Cloudflare's only education-adjacent offering is free learning content/certification programs (Learning Hub / CF-ICD), which is training, not hosting/PaaS — and its landing page (developers.cloudflare.com/learning-hub) was 404, so status is unverified. 2025–2026 material changes affecting the free tiers: Pages is not sunset but is being pushed into a migrate-to-Workers posture (a 'Migrate From Pages' guide and a Pages index note steering users to Workers static assets; Pages limits unchanged: 500 builds/mo, 100 projects, 20,000 files); Workers Logs free tier migrates to Cloudflare Observability pricing on 2026-12-01; Durable Objects SQLite storage billing begins January 2026; legacy KV API routes deprecated with 2026-10-15 migration deadline; R2 free tier (10 GB) unchanged, with new free sub-tiers added in 2026 for R2 SQL (10 GB scanned/mo) and R2 Data Catalog (1M ops + 10 GB compaction/mo), both billing-enabled 2026-08-03. Note: the WebSearch budget was exhausted for this session, so 2025-2026 change verification relied on official docs/changelog pages (fetched today) rather than press/news coverage; 'last updated' dates on the source pages are 2026, confirming the limits above are current.
- **2025–26 change:** 2024-2026 changes: (1) Startup program was revamped Sep 2024 from a one-size $500k-era offer to a credits system capped at $250,000 (3 tiers). April 11, 2025: expanded to four tiers — $5,000 (bootstrapped/stealth, founded last 5 yrs), $25,000 (funded to $1M), $100,000 ($1M-$5M + approved VC/accelerator), $250,000 (Tier-1 VCs / AI / Workers Launchpad) — with a 5-year founding window and a 'BOOTSTRAPPED' promo-code path. The current 2026 page replaced this with three tiers ($10k/$100k/$350k), a 10-year founding window, 'funded up to Series B within the last 12 months', a hard payment-method-on-file requirement, and per-tier Workers AI caps. Old $250k/$5k-$250k four-tier copy still lingers on stale localized sub-pages (en-gb, en-ca) — treat cloudflare.com/startups as current. (2) Pages platform shift 2025-2026: Pages was consolidated into the Workers platform; docs now steer new projects to Workers ('Start new projects with Workers'; Pages Functions billed exactly as Workers), but Pages is not sunset — Free plan numbers stand as verified Sep 5, 2026 doc: $0, unlimited static requests/bandwidth, 500 builds/mo, 100 projects, 100 custom domains/project, 20,000 files (raised from the prior 10,000 cap; paid tiers unlock 100,000 via PAGES_WRANGLER_MAJOR_VERSION=4), 25 MiB max asset. (3) No student/education program was created or sunset in this window; 'Project Cybersafe Schools' (free K-12 email security + DNS filtering, US districts) remains the only education-facing free offer. No trial-credit program (e.g., a $500 API-credit trial) is currently advertised on the official pages I read.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025–2026 changes: (1) Startup Program tier structure was revamped repeatedly: Sept 2024 revamp set the ceiling at $250k; April 2025 expanded to 4 tiers ($5k/$25k/$100k/$250k); by mid-2026 it was restructured into 3 tiers ($10k/$100k/$350k, per the current cloudflare.com/startups page and a May 2026 announcement). (2) Sep 2025: non-profit, civil-society, and public-interest organizations became eligible for the Startup program (up to $250k) — application window opened with a Dec 1 close for that cohort. (3) Eligibility window widened from 'founded within last 5 years' (2019–2024) to 'founded within last 10 years.' (4) Workers free-plan subrequest limit changed Feb 2026: now 50 external + 1,000 Cloudflare-service subrequests per invocation (previously a flat 1,000 total). (5) Workers Logs: effective Dec 1, 2026 it moves to Cloudflare Observability pricing (a near-term change affecting the free observability front). (6) Sep 2026: Free-account creation expanded so users can create up to 5 standalone Free accounts via dashboard or API. No traditional 'education license discount' or separate individual developer-credit grant exists at Cloudflare; the free value for students is the perpetual Free plan + free Academy + discretionary Ambassador/Community grants, and for startups the credits program.
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes: (1) Startup program tiers were "recently updated" (undated on page, current as of 2026-10-22): Tier 3 raised $5,000→$10,000/yr and Tier 1 $250,000→$350,000/yr — older references to a "$5K credit" are stale; entry tier is now $10K. (2) No general-public time-boxed trial or one-off trial-credit offer exists; the only dollar-credit offer is the application-based startup program (perpetual free tiers are not trials and never expire). (3) New billable dimensions landed on the free platform in 2026: R2 SQL and R2 Data Catalog billing enabled 2026-08-03 for non-Enterprise accounts (free tier 10 GB scanned / 1M catalog ops / 10 GB compaction per month), AI Search billing begins 2026-11-01 (free: 5M ingestion tokens, 10 GB-month, 1K semantic + 1K full-text queries/month), and 2026-02-11 raised Workers subrequest limits (Free stays at 50 external/1,000 internal per invocation; Paid default 10,000, configurable to 10M) — free accounts can still accrue charges on metered add-ons, so set spend limits. (4) 2025-12-05 WAF max-payload change: Free zones already protected to 1 MB; paid plans need support ticket to enable. (5) Unbound/Bundled Workers plans are deprecated and unavailable to new accounts. (6) Education: no .edu-only program found and Cloudflare Academy's host (academy.cloudflare.com) did not resolve today — treat its current availability as unverified; if it was sunset in 2025-2026, no student/education free offer remains at Cloudflare.

### DigitalOcean

- **DigitalOcean Startups program (formerly Hatch) — up to $100,000 in compute credits over 12 months + 15 months free Standard-tier support** (high) — DigitalOcean Startups (rebranded from 'Hatch', program running since 2016). Official program page (read 2026-10-22): '12 months to scale' — credits for 12 months, amount varies by partner and is stated in the onboarding email, with a $10,000 monthly limit and charges for any monthly use above it. January 22, 2025 press release (investors.digitalocean.com, official) states 'up to $100,000 in Compute Credits' plus 'Complimentary Premium Support' and marketplace access to 600,000+ customers; BusinessWire's release also stated 'up to 3 Months of Free GPU Usage' for qualified startups. Current page lists 'Free access to Standard-tier paid support for 15 months.' Official ToS (last updated May 11, 2026) confirms a 12-month program term and 1-year credit window. Enrollment itself is free ('no fee to participate'); non-transferable credits.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have raised $10M or less total funding. Requires a company website with a matching company-domain email (no Gmail etc.) and a new DigitalOcean team account on that email. A valid credit card IS required on the account (for billing/overages). Startups that have previously used DigitalOcean credits are excluded. Service-based businesses (consulting, web/app dev shops, marketing agencies) are ineligible. AI-native startups are prioritized but not required. No .edu requirement. No published country restriction; apply through a partner (VC/accelerator/incubator, e.g. Techstars) or directly via the application form ('Other').
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a NEW DigitalOcean team account with a company-domain email (free-mail not accepted) and a valid credit card; 2) Apply via digitalocean.com/startups / do.co/AIStartups — through an approved partner (accelerator/VC such as Techstars) or directly by selecting 'Other'; 3) On acceptance the credit amount is set per partner and stated in the onboarding email; 4) Spend within the 12-month window, max $10,000 usable per month. 15 months of Standard-tier support and the Welcome Kit partner perks (Stripe Atlas 20% off, QuickNode up to $5k, Airtable $2,000, 100ms $2,500, Miro $1,000, Notion $1,000, Cloud66 $500, Bolster $1,000, Mercury $500 cash on $50k deposit, HubSpot up to 90% off year one, ChargeBee free until $1M revenue, Shortcut 2 months free, Ovvy 90 days free) are included on acceptance.
  - *Expiry:* Credits expire 12 months after DigitalOcean accepts you into the program (one year from acceptance per ToS, §1.2); unused credits are forfeited with no extension or rollover. Free Standard-tier support lasts 15 months. ToS auto-terminates the arrangement on acquisition, IPO, insolvency, or ceasing normal operations; liability capped at $100; no resell/sublicense; crypto mining prohibited.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card required at signup — not 'free' in the no-CC sense. Core credits as of May 13, 2026 explicitly EXCLUDE: GPU Droplets, NVIDIA H100 GPU Kubernetes, Bare Metal GPUs, Dedicated Inference, Serverless Inference/third-party AI models, Paperspace Machines, Cloudways, and third-party pass-through charges — GPU access is a separate invite-only benefit, and the January 2025 'up to 3 months free GPU' is no longer published. Amount is unpublished and partner-set; the $100,000 ceiling comes only from the Jan 2025 press release. Usage above $10,000 in a month is billed to your card. Powered-off Droplets still bill. Egress beyond bundled allowances is charged separately.
  - *Source:* https://www.digitalocean.com/startups

Credit card required at signup (a ~$1 temporary pre-authorization is taken and reversed). Stopped Droplets still bill (resources stay reserved) — only destroying them stops charges.

Free allowances are per-resource (e.g., 500 GiB outbound is per Droplet plan; extra egress beyond bundled allowances is billed — list rate is ~$0.01/GiB per third-party pricing trackers, unverified on the official page today). Credits cover nearly all DO services (Droplets/VPS, App Platform, Kubernetes, Spaces, managed databases, CPU bare metal) but as of 2026-05-13 EXCLUDE: GPU Droplets, NVIDIA H100 GPU Kubernetes, Bare Metal GPUs, Dedicated Inference, Serverless Inference, third-party AI models outside DO infrastructure (incl. Anthropic/OpenAI), Paperspace Machines, Cloudways, and pass-through third-party charges. GPU credits are a separate, by-invitation benefit ('Early-Stage Boost'). Also includes 15 months of free Standard-tier paid support, expert 1:1s with DO PMs/engineers, Marketplace access, and a Welcome Kit partner perks: Stripe Atlas 20% off, Shortcut 2 months free, Cloud66 $500, Ovvy 90 days free, QuickNode up to $5,000, Bolster $1,000, Mercury $500 cash on $50k deposit, Airtable $2,000, Notion $1,000, HubSpot up to 90% off year one, Miro $1,000, Chargebee free until $1M revenue, 100ms $2,500. No .edu or enrollment requirement.

GPU/inference/third-party AI models are excluded as of 2026-05-13 — a major gotcha for AI workloads. Overage beyond the monthly cap and beyond Droplet bandwidth allowances is billed. Powered-off Droplets still bill. Egress beyond the included bandwidth allowance costs extra. No free trial exists for bare-metal or dedicated servers; they are covered only if your credits apply and usage stays under the monthly cap (bare-metal CPUs eligible; bare-metal GPUs excluded).

The credit applies to all products except SaaS Add-Ons — so it covers Droplets (VPS), bare metal and dedicated servers, App Platform, etc. A valid payment method must be on file before you can create resources; your card is charged only after the credit is exhausted or the 90-day window expires ('if you received a $5 credit for 90 days, then that $5 credit is automatically applied to your account' — pricing FAQ). Credit expires 90 days after signup; it cannot be transferred to other teams. Governing 'Promotional Credit & Discount Terms' (last updated Jun 7 2024): no cash value, non-transferable, no stacking with other promotions, exclusions for Marketplace/Support/crypto mining, voiding for accounts created solely to obtain credits. Docs page last verified 15 Jul 2026.

No region limits stated on the page.

Overage beyond $5 (or any spend after day 90) bills normally; free outbound bandwidth allowance (e.g., 500 GiB/mo on small Droplets) and free reserved IP/DNS are separate product inclusions, not credit. Fraud/accounts created solely for credits are voided.

The URL requested in the task (digitalocean.com/community/solutions/digitalocean-for-startups) and a guessed .../digitalocean-for-students both return HTTP 404; the startup program now lives at digitalocean.com/startups. No .edu email requirement appears anywhere in official pages. 2) Program rebrand + duration change: 'Hatch' was rebranded 'DigitalOcean Startups' with a new 2025 benefits package announced Jan 22, 2025 (investors.digitalocean.com press release + BusinessWire). The previously referenced 'up to $100,000 over 24 months' is superseded: the official ToS (last updated May 11, 2026) sets a 12-month program term and a 1-year credit window, and the current page states amounts 'vary' per partner with a $10,000 monthly limit. 3) May 13, 2026 restriction expansion: core startup credits now explicitly exclude GPU Droplets, NVIDIA H100 GPU Kubernetes, Bare Metal GPUs, Dedicated Inference, Serverless Inference/third-party AI models, Paperspace Machines and Cloudways (previously only GPU Droplets and H100 products were excluded); GPU support moved fully to the invite-only 'Early-Stage Boost' packages, and the Jan 2025 'up to 3 months free GPU usage' language is no longer published. 4) Support wording changed: 'Complimentary Premium Support' (Jan 2025 release) is now '15 months of free Standard-tier support'. 5) The older $200/60-day new-account trial no longer appears on the official pricing page or docs (docs.digitalocean.com/resources/free-trial/ 404s); the current trial is a $5/90-day credit automatically applied to a new account. 6) Oct 29, 2025: AI Startup Ecosystem launched with Techstars as founding partner — no published amounts, funnels into the Startups program.
- **2025–26 change:** Major 2025-2026 changes: (1) HATCH RETIRED — the Hatch brand was retired and replaced by the "DigitalOcean Startups" program; the Hatch ToS now only point to a prior version of the Startups ToS (Startups ToS last updated May 11, 2026; third-party guides place the brand retirement on May 11, 2026). The Jan 2025 Hatch offer of "up to $100,000 in compute credits + up to 3 months free GPU usage" (announced Jan 22, 2025) is no longer the current offer — the current page says credit amount 'varies' with a $10,000/month cap and unpublished totals. (2) STUDENT CREDIT ENDED — DigitalOcean exited the GitHub Student Developer Pack; students were told around July 1, 2026 that DO was "winding down the program" and "ending their participation in the GitHub Student Developer Pack"; ALL Student Pack promotional credits, including ones already redeemed that had run a full year, expired universally on July 31, 2026 (retroactive cutoff). From June 8, 2026 the credit had already excluded GPU Droplets, H100 Kubernetes, Bare Metal GPUs, DO Inference, third-party AI models, Paperspace and Cloudways. There is no replacement DigitalOcean student/education license as of 2026-10-22. (3) CREDIT COVERAGE NARROWED — as of May 13, 2026 the Startups program credits explicitly exclude Dedicated/Serverless Inference, third-party AI models, Paperspace Machines, Cloudways and Bare Metal GPUs (in addition to the earlier GPU Droplet/H100 exclusions), and the page now states the $10,000/month cap. (4) A limit reduction was recorded around 2026-04-07 (narrowed signup-credit terms; low confidence, from a third-party changelog). (5) No free bare-metal or dedicated-server trial, no perpetual free VPS tier, and no no-credit-card offer exists as of 2026-10-22 — a payment card is required for every DO offer above.
- **2025–26 change:** Sunset/change notes for 2025–2026: (1) The former $200/60-day new-user trial is wound down — the old trial landing pages (/free-trial/ and /trial/) now 404 (verified today), and it has been replaced by the small automatic $5/90-day signup credit applied to the first team (docs page last verified 15 Jul 2026). (2) Startup Program: effective May 13 2026, credits were re-scoped to EXCLUDE GPU Droplets, NVIDIA H100 GPU Kubernetes, Bare Metal GPUs, Dedicated Inference, Serverless Inference, third-party AI models (Anthropic/OpenAI), Paperspace and Cloudways — so startup credits no longer fund GPU/bare-metal AI hardware; program also explicitly grants no extensions and forfeits unused credits. (3) Wavemakers is DigitalOcean's current community-chapter program (replacing/continuing the older 'DigitalOcean Community Chapter' branding) with unpublished credit amounts. (4) No evidence of any education/student program in the 2025–2026 official site; the Promotional Credit & Discount Terms page is unchanged since June 7 2024. Method caveat: WebSearch was unavailable for this session (shared budget exhausted), so the '2025-2026 changes' cross-check relied solely on WebFetch of DigitalOcean's official pages; third-party press coverage was not verified.
- **2025–26 change:** GitHub Student Developer Pack $200 student credit: DigitalOcean is no longer listed as a perk in the current GitHub Student Developer Pack (checked 2026-10-22 via education.github.com/pack), and the dedicated DigitalOcean /github-students page now redirects to the generic homepage. Community reports (May 2026) say the credit stopped applying after GitHub authorization + adding a card. Treat the 'known so far' $200 GitHub-pack student credit as outdated/unavailable; the only live student-accessible path today is the general $200/60-day new-account trial.DO Startups credit framing changed materially: earlier 'known so far' cited 'up to $250K in credits.' The current official page (checked 2026-10-22) does NOT print a headline total; it frames the benefit as a pre-determined amount ('varies', via onboarding email) with a $10,000 monthly ceiling over 12 months (max ~$120K of core credits), and the eligible-service list was broadened effective May 13, 2026. The $250K figure is unsupported by the current official page.The official /free-trial/ landing page returns a 404 today, so the $200/60-day trial could not be read on a live official page; it remains corroborated by current (2026) third-party guides and first-party DigitalOcean community threads. No active .edu-only education license program was found.
- **Critic correction** (Standing new-account credit is ~$5 for 90 days ...; the former $200/60-day ...): DigitalOcean's new-account promotion is $200 in credit for the first 60 days, new accounts only, per its promo text on community pages. The $5 for 90 days is a separate AI-inference trial. My homepage fetch showed no banner, so this rests on the community-page text and third-party listings.
- **Critic correction** (conflicting: '$200 / 60-day' claim versus official 90-day signup-credit expiry): The official signup-credit doc (last verified 2026-07-15) says credits expire 90 days after signup and a payment method is needed before creating Droplets. The Free Trial entry says about $5 for 90 days, while the student/startup entry still says $200 for 60 days. The official excerpt read did not state an amount, so confirm it there. A secondary source (October 2026) says the GitHub Student Pack credit ended 2026-07-31; that is unverified and the $200 student claim needs re-checking.

### Google Cloud

- **Google Cloud Free Trial — $300 welcome credit (90 days)** (high) — New customers get $300 free credit, valid 90 days from Free Trial billing account creation. Credit covers any product including overages beyond Free Tier limits; it does not roll over (expires 90 days from signup even if you upgrade to paid). Free Trial billing account auto-closes when the $300 is spent or 90 days pass unless you manually upgrade to a Paid billing account (then you keep remaining credit until the 90-day mark and keep Free Tier access). Eligibility: you must have NEVER been a paying user of Google Cloud, Google Maps Platform, or Firebase, and never signed up for the Free Trial before (one per person/billing profile). GPUs, Windows servers and some features are not covered. During trial you are not billed — only if you manually upgrade to paid do charges begin. 30-day data-export window after the trial ends. Compute Engine trial restricted to 8 concurrent cores.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, any country with GCP access (no .edu or startup status needed). Credit card/other payment method REQUIRED at signup for identity verification (Google may run a pending authorization, not a charge). US/Mexico government entities excluded.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.google.com (Get started for free) -> add card -> $300 credit is preloaded into the Free Trial billing account automatically; spend over 90 days on any product incl. Vertex AI/Gemini API; optionally click Upgrade in console to keep credit alive beyond trial features. Do NOT click 'Activate a paid account' unless you want charges.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days from signup or until $300 is exhausted, whichever first; unused credit does not survive account closure. Free Tier usage continues if you convert to a paid account.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs Free Tier: the $300 is for trial billing only; Free Tier allowances are separate, not charged against it. CC required but you are only charged if you manually upgrade to paid. Egress, third-party models (e.g. non-Google LLMs on Vertex) cost above free allowances. 30-day migration window after end; GCE resources may be deleted and are not recoverable.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google Cloud Free Tier — 20+ always-free products (no expiry)** (high) — Per-product monthly free allowances, reset each month, do NOT expire but limits are 'subject to change'. Verified examples from cloud.google.com/free: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro/month; Cloud Storage 5 GB Standard; BigQuery 1 TB scanned/month; Cloud Run 2M requests + Cloud Run Functions 2M invocations/month; GKE 1 Autopilot/zonal cluster; Pub/Sub 10 GB messages; Vision AI 1,000 units; Speech-to-Text 60 min; Natural Language 5,000 units; Cloud Build 120 build-minutes/day; KMS Autokey 100 active key versions + 10k ops; Artifact Registry, Firestore 1 GB, Secret Manager 6 versions, Cloud Shell 5 GB. Includes 'AI APIs' free usage — the GCP-hosted AI endpoints (Vertex AI GenAI preview limits apply separately).
  - *Eligibility:* All new GCP users (not student-only). A Google Cloud billing account with a registered payment method is required even to use the Free Tier ('eligible customers' in a paid or trial billing account).
  - *How to claim:* Any GCP account (trial or paid) automatically gets the free monthly allowances; no separate claim. Sign up at cloud.google.com with a card.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry (renews monthly), but usage resets on the 1st; unused allowance does not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Overspending beyond free limits is billed pay-as-you-go to your card. Free Tier products are region/feature-specific (e.g., e2-micro, us-central1 historically); GPU workloads are not in the free tier. Third-party LLMs on Vertex AI are billed. 'Not charged against $300 credit' means free-tier usage is on top of trial credit.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Gemini API / AI Studio Free tier — free LLM access, no credit card** (medium) — The Gemini Developer API has a Free tier: free input/output tokens with limited access to certain models, plus free Google AI Studio usage; no billing account or card required (Free tier qualification = 'Active project or free trial'). Per the current official docs, exact RPM/TPM/RPD are now model-specific and only viewable in aistudio.google.com/rate-limit (docs stopped publishing the static table). As of mid-2025 community-referenced Free-tier limits were ~Gemini 2.5 Pro 5 RPM / 250K TPM / 100 RPD, 2.5 Flash 10/250K/250, 2.5 Flash-Lite 15/250K/1,000. Pay tiers: Tier 1 needs a linked billing account (cap $250/min-rolling), Tier 2 after $100 spent + 3 days ($2,000 cap), Tier 3 after $1,000 + 30 days ($20k-100k cap). Upgrading to paid now requires prepaying min $5. Flagship 'Gemini 4 Argon' is NOT available on the Free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a Google account; no card, no .edu. Region: available in most countries; Google AI Studio usage is free in all available countries.
  - *How to claim:* Go to aistudio.google.com -> create project -> generate an API key -> call Gemini models within Free-tier limits. For deploying up to 2 full-stack apps without GCP billing, use the Google Cloud 'Starter Tier' (AI Studio -> Deploy, eligible accounts only).
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite while the Free tier exists (no renewal); limits subject to change; RPD resets midnight Pacific.
  - *Gotchas:* Free-tier content/prompts may be used by Google to improve its products (Paid tier does not); newest flagship models excluded from Free tier; limits are per-project not per-key; from June 19, 2026 the Gemini API stops accepting requests from unrestricted API keys (must restrict keys to the Gemini API endpoint); '429' when hitting limits; AI Studio playground daily caps have been tightened in 2026 (community reports ~10-30 req/day for top models under capacity pressure).
  - *Source:* https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/rate-limits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Start tier: up to $2,000 credits / 1 year (self-serve)** (high) — For digital-native tech startups with a working MVP planning to seek venture funding. Benefits verified from the official benefits page: up to $2,000 USD GCP credits valid for 1 year; $200 Google Skills training credits; Google Workspace Business Plus free 12 months (new signups); access to partner Startup Perks (e.g. Datadog, ElevenLabs 33M credits, Render $500, GitLab Ultimate 20 licenses) — the ones starred are Scale-only.
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; not yet received Google Cloud credits (beyond the free trial); tech startup that plans to raise VC funding; clear product roadmap. No funding cap (pre-funding/bootstrapped is fine). Decision at Google's discretion. Company email matching public domain expected; GCP account + billing ID needed.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the Google for Startups Cloud Program application form at cloud.google.com/startup (self-serve 'Apply now') — or via the Startups Perks/partner pages. Complete the onboarding checklist at cloud.google.com/startup/onboarding (billing account setup) so credits attach to the correct billing account.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid 1 year from issuance. Program itself is ongoing (self-serve available).
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a GCP billing account (payment method on file; credits offset charges, card isn't used if spend <= credit). Third-party (non-Google) models on Vertex are billed directly, not covered. 'Beyond the free trial' means the $300 trial doesn't disqualify you.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Scale tier: up to $200,000 credits over 2 years ($350,000 for AI-first startups)** (high) — Year 1: 100% of GCP spend up to $100,000 in credits (AI-first startups: up to $250,000). Year 2: 20% of GCP spend up to an additional $100,000 (both tracks). Total: up to $200,000, or up to $350,000 for AI-first startups using/planning to use Gemini as a foundation of their product. Credits cover Google models (Gemini, Gemma); third-party models billed separately. Also: up to $12,000 Enhanced Support credits (1 year, 1-hr critical response), $500 Google Skills credits, $600/month Google Maps Platform usage credits (separate application), Workspace Business Plus free 12 months, dedicated Startup Success Manager + Customer Engineers, and starred Scale-only partner perks (ElevenLabs 33M, Datadog up to $100k, OpenSearch $100k+30k, Render up to $100k, etc.).
  - *Eligibility:* VC-funded (equity funding Pre-Seed/Seed within last 5 years; Series A within last 12 months); founded within the last 5 years; not yet received more than $5,000 in GCP credits; stealth mode OK with funding verification. AI track additionally requires Gemini (or planned Gemini use) as foundation of primary product. No explicit revenue cap; decision at Google's discretion.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup ('Apply now') or apply through a Google Cloud partner (VCs/accelerators: Y Combinator — dedicated GPU/TPU access + Scale-tier benefits for AI cohorts; 500 Startups, AI2, ERA, StartX, Berkeley Skydeck). Approval is reviewed; YC and similar partners get high acceptance rates.
  - *Expiry:* Credits structured as Year-1 match + Year-2 20% top-up, 2-year program window; no renewal beyond that (case-by-case requests exist via partners).
  - *Gotchas:* To hit the $100k Year-2 top-up you must actually spend $500k in GCP in Year 2 — it's a match, not a grant. Year-1 '100%' also means real spend must occur. Third-party LLMs excluded from credit coverage. GPU/TPU access in partner cohorts may be ad-hoc scheduling, not reserved capacity. Maps and some perks need separate applications.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/ai
- **Google Cloud for Education — student & faculty course credits (no credit card)** (medium) — Faculty at regionally-accredited US institutions (or degree-awarding universities in eligible countries; for-profit institutions excluded) apply via a form and receive GCP credit coupons: faculty get their own, and a URL is emailed that students open to verify student status (school/college email confirmation) and receive coupon codes. Credits land in per-course Cloud Billing accounts. Official help docs state the program 'does not require a credit card to sign up'. Credits valid for 12 months after the course start date / 12 months after redemption; Free Tier usage does not count against them; GCP credits may not be used for crypto mining; GPU access must be requested. Separate 'training credits': current students can request 200 free Google Skills credits (online labs, courses, badges; credits expire 1 year after issue; request processing up to 2 weeks).
  - *Eligibility:* .edu / school email of an eligible institution (regionally accredited in US; degree-granting in eligible countries abroad). Students get credits only through a course whose faculty applied. No funding or startup criteria. Amount per course varies (historically ~$50+ per course; Google awards at its discretion).
  - *How to claim:* Faculty: apply via the Google Cloud for Education form (cloud.google.com/edu). Students: use the invitation URL your faculty sends -> confirm school email -> redeem coupon code in the Google Cloud console (Billing -> Coupons). For Google Skills: request the 200 training credits via the training-credits form linked from cloud.google.com/edu/students.
  - *Expiry:* GCP course credits: valid 12 months from course start/redemption; per-course billing accounts. Google Skills credits: expire 1 year after issued.
  - *Gotchas:* Student credits are tied to faculty applications — there is no longer a self-serve $300/12-month 'student credit' grant on the official pages (the older student program appears sunset; see sunset notes). Credits are non-commercial, for learning. GPU/CPU/project quotas capped; requests may be denied. School email must be verifiable; .edu generators violate ToS.
  - *Source:* https://support.google.com/google-cloud-higher-ed/answer/10324788
- **Google Cloud Free Trial / program status as of 2026-10-22** (high) — No sunset: the $300/90-day Free Trial and the Free Tier remain current as of this date (pages updated 2025-2026, terms archived 2025-01-30). No new standalone free LLM 'trial credit' has been announced in 2025-2026; the AI offer is the Gemini API Free tier (no card) plus free-tier products. Startup program remains two tiers (Start/Scale) with the 2024 restructured amounts. Gemini API added spend-based tier caps and (in 2026) mandatory API-key restriction and Prepay min $5 for paid tiers.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a (status note)
  - *How to claim:* n/a
  - *Expiry:* n/a
  - *Gotchas:* Treat static free-tier RPM/RPD numbers as volatile — Google moved them to a per-account dashboard in 2026.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/archive/terms/free-trial-20250130
- **Google Cloud Free Tier (always-free monthly quotas, 20+ products)** (high) — Free-forever monthly usage on select GCP products, e.g.: Compute Engine 1 e2-micro VM/month (us-west1/us-central1/us-east1; time-based across those regions) + 30GB standard persistent disk + 1GB egress; Cloud Storage 5GB US regional storage, 5,000 Class A / 50,000 Class B ops, 100GB NA egress; Cloud Run 2M requests, 360,000 GB-s memory & 180,000 vCPU-s, 1GB NA egress; Cloud Run functions 2M invocations; BigQuery 1TiB queries + 10GiB storage/month; App Engine Standard 28 F1-h/day + 9 B1-h/day + 1GB egress/day; Firestore 1GiB, 50k reads/20k writes/20k deletes per day; GKE 1 cluster charge/month; GCS egress and other egress outside NA or beyond limits bill at standard rates. GPUs/TPUs never free.
  - *Eligibility:* Requires an active GCP billing account (Paid or Free Trial) in good standing; no negotiated pricing contract. No .edu or age requirement; effectively free tier is available to any GCP account holder. Free-tier benefits on Cloud Storage apply only to us-east1/us-west1/us-central1.
  - *How to claim:* Create any GCP billing account (usually via the $300 Free Trial signup, which requires a card); stay within per-product monthly quotas and you are never billed. Do not create paid resources beyond quotas, or overage bills on your card.
  - *Expiry:* No end date, but Google reserves the right to change or eliminate limits with 30 days' advance notice; quotas do not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Common trap: creating a GCP account for 'free tier' still typically needs a card (Free Trial signup requires one); egress, cross-region traffic, GPU/TPU, and overage are billed; limits are per billing account.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Start tier ($2,000 GCP credits)** (high) — For 'digital-native' startups with a working MVP and plans to seek venture funding soon: $2,000 in GCP credits, valid 12 months (applies to GCP services and select GCP offerings; covers Google's proprietary models — program credits do NOT cover third-party models, which are billed directly). Also included: free-tier AI Studio (Generative AI Studio) access, 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free (new signups), $200 Google Skills training credit, and 'Startup Perks' (partner discounts/offers).
  - *Eligibility:* Founded within the last 24 months; has NOT yet received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial; digital-native tech startup with working MVP + clear business model + plans to seek VC funding soon. Excluded: service agencies, consultancies, traditional small businesses; companies that have IPO'd or been acquired; educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content companies, dev shops, crypto mining companies, token distributors contrary to regulatory guidance. Acceptance at Google Cloud's discretion. No VC funding required at this tier (it's the pre-funded stage).
  - *How to claim:* Apply self-serve at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (per-tier page: cloud.google.com/startup/pre-seed). You must sign in with a Google account whose business email matches your public website domain, and provide your 18-character GCP billing account ID (create a GCP billing account if you don't have one); Google reviews (most decisions within a few business days); credits are deposited to your billing account.
  - *Expiry:* GCP credits: 12 months from issuance. Workspace Business Plus: 12 months. Google Skills $200 credit: training credits; expiration not stated on the Start page (student program credits expire 1 year after issuance — treat similarly). No renewal; re-applications case-by-case via cloudstartupsupport@google.com.
  - *Gotchas:* Start-tier page does not state a credit-card requirement, but you need an existing GCP billing account to receive credits, and creating a new GCP account/payment setup typically requires a payment method (Free Trial signup definitely does, per docs). Workspace benefit: your company domain must not have been on a paid Google Workspace plan within 31 days of applying, and the benefit cannot be applied to annual Workspace plans. Credits excluded from Marketplace/third-party products; egress/networking costs beyond free tiers still billable.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/pre-seed
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program — Scale tier (up to $200,000 GCP credits over 2 years; $350,000 for AI-first)** (high) — VC-funded early-stage startups get up to $200,000 in GCP credits over two years: Year 1 = 100% credit up to $100,000; Year 2 = 20% credit on year-2 spend, up to an additional $100,000. AI-first startups: Year 1 = 100% up to $250,000, Year 2 = 20% up to +$100,000 → up to $350,000 total (equivalently, 'qualifying venture-backed startups can tap an additional $150,000 with the AI startup program' on top of the $200K base). Program credits cover Google's models (Gemini, Gemma); third-party models billed directly and not covered. Also: up to $12,000 Enhanced Support credits for one year (1-hour critical response), 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free, $600/month Google Maps Platform credits for one year (separate application), $500 Google Skills training credits, dedicated Startup Success Manager + Startup Customer Engineers, Web3 program access, and partner perks.
  - *Eligibility:* Must have received 'recent startup equity funding' (pre-seed to Series A by an institutional investor/VC, or common Web3 funding sources — SAFE agreements and verifiable token raises/blockchain foundation grants count; definition: Pre-Seed/Seed rounds within the last 5 years, Series A rounds within the last 12 months). Founded within the last 5 years (stealth mode OK with investor-verified funding). Must not have received more than $5,000 in GCP credits already. Not eligible: IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government entities, nonprofits, personal blogs/content, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, crypto-mining companies, token distributors against regulatory guidance; private-equity, government-innovation-grant, crowdfunding, angel and friends-&-family funding do NOT qualify for Scale tier (may be evaluated for Start tier instead).
  - *How to claim:* Apply self-serve at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (per-tier page: cloud.google.com/startup/seed-series-a). Sign in with the company business email matching your public website domain + provide the 18-character GCP billing account ID + verification of funding (public sources; stealth: work with your investor). Decision usually within a few business days (manual review may take longer); credits deposit to your billing account and you receive an in-console benefit tracker.
  - *Expiry:* Year-1 credits usable in year 1; year-2 credits (20% of spend, capped +$100K) accrue in year 2 → program spans 2 years. Workspace 12 months; Maps $600/month for 1 year (separate app); Enhanced Support credits valid 1 year.
  - *Gotchas:* Year-2 benefit is a 20% discount-style credit on actual year-2 spend (max $100K) — it is not a pre-funded $100K; you must keep spending. Credits exclude Marketplace third-party offerings, third-party AI models, and per footnote only 'GCP Services and Select GCP offerings'. Workspace 31-day paid-plan rule applies. Google 'unable to accept' a long exclusion list (see eligibility); acceptance at Google's discretion. AI tier requires being genuinely 'AI-first' (AI in foundational structure/strategy, not a bolt-on) — some applicants get rejected even if they say AI. Data/egress: credits offset compute/AI usage, but egress and overage still bill if credits exhaust.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/seed-series-a
- **Startup program add-ons announced 2025–2026: 'New: AI benefits' ($350K ceiling), Web3 benefits, ISV Startup Springboard, Gemini Startup Forum** (medium) — The benefits page now labels 'New: AI benefits' (AI webinars/content, dedicated AI training, resources, experts, access to up to $350,000 in credits) and 'New: Web3 benefits' (Web3 Perks: partner offers from Quicknode, TRM, Allium and more). FAQ: Web3 startups get 'up to $200,000 USD over two years for Google Cloud and Firebase usage' plus Discord channel, foundation grants, VIP event access. Scale-tier page adds ISV Startup Springboard (12-week program for emerging AI startups: bespoke technical guidance + GTM resources) and the Gemini Startup Forum (Google for Startups + DeepMind + Cloud; curated in-person gatherings for seed–Series A founders). Benefits page also lists a 'Future of AI: Perspectives on generative media for startups' report and Cloud Startup Customer Engineers with faster response times for critical issues.
  - *Eligibility:* AI tier: Scale-tier VC-backed startups that are AI-first (AI foundational to product/strategy). Web3: startups with verifiable token raises / blockchain foundation grants (SAFE also eligible). ISV Springboard: emerging AI startups in the program. Forum: seed-to-Series A founders (invitation/curated).
  - *How to claim:* No separate application — benefits come with Scale-tier approval; Web3 program has its own qualification via the same application (flag yourself as Web3); Springboard/Forum through program onboarding. Re-applications or questions: cloudstartupsupport@google.com.
  - *Expiry:* AI-credits structure (Y1 $250K 100% + Y2 20% up to $100K) spans 2 years; Web3 $200K over 2 years; Springboard is a 12-week cohort.
  - *Gotchas:* Web3 'up to $200,000' covers Google Cloud AND Firebase (distinct from the $350K AI cloud-credits ceiling — these are different track framings). Third-party models still billed direct. AI-first qualification is subjective and at Google's discretion; being an 'AI company' in name isn't enough (FAQ example: another AI startup founder did not qualify).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/faq
- **Google Cloud for Education — faculty teaching credits (up to $100 per teaching staff + up to $50 per student)** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for GCP credits for classroom use: up to $100 in GCP credits per teaching staff and up to $50 in GCP credits per student, so students get hands-on cloud experience at no cost. Application is limited to faculty (students cannot apply for their own teaching credits; they can encourage faculty to apply for their next course). Course start date must be no more than 1 year out; approved credits are redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after the course start date, and are good for up to 1 year after course start. Credits attach to specified school-issued email domains and are non-transferable. Faculty Community access is restricted to verified/approved faculty. Also linked: Career Launchpad program (coach students through Google Cloud certificates/credentials) and Google DeepMind AI Research Foundations curriculum.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only, at an academic institution; requires official school email with a valid .edu (or equivalent institutional) domain ('Please use your official school email address with a valid edu domain'; if none, you may nominate another address, credits attach to it and are non-transferable); must provide faculty directory profile link. Country restriction: the teaching program is 'currently only available in' a listed set of ~60 countries (Argentina, Australia, Canada, Germany, India, Japan, Nigeria, UK, US, etc. — the country dropdown on the form; if your country is absent you can only 'express interest').
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching/ (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/faculty → 'Apply for Cloud credits'). Fill personal info with school .edu email, country, institution, faculty directory link, course details (name, start date, abstract, course URL) and member info (all school-issued domains, expected students/TA-staff count). Check spam for CloudEduGrants@google.com confirmations; redeem credits via the education-credits redemption guide once approved.
  - *Expiry:* Credits good for up to 1 year after course start; redeemable window = approval through 16 weeks after course start; course start must be within 1 year of application.
  - *Gotchas:* Not open to students directly (students must go through faculty). .edu/institutional email effectively required (nomination of alternate email loses the .edu gate but credits stay non-transferable). Country list is restrictive — not all countries served. Small amounts ($100/$50) — intended for learning, not production. Students should avoid accidentally signing up for the GCP Free Trial with personal cards in classroom contexts (Google's own faculty guide warns of this).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Google Cloud for Education — student training credits ($200 Google Skills credits) and free labs/badges** (high) — Students can request $200 in Google Skills (formerly Cloud Skills Boost) training credits, which expire one year after issuance, plus free access to online hands-on labs, skill badges, and courses; Google Skills Arcade (gamified challenges with badges) is free. The student page also points to DSC (Developer Student Clubs) and the Career Launchpad pathway. This is training credit, not GCP usage credit — students do not get a separate GCP-usage student credit program (the individual $300 Free Trial is the only personal usage credit, and it requires a card).
  - *Eligibility:* Students (individuals); request via the student training-credits form (services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudskillsbooststudenttrainingcreditsapplication/). No .edu gate stated on the page, but it's a student-facing learning program; free labs/courses also available without the credit.
  - *How to claim:* On cloud.google.com/edu/students click 'Request credits' → complete the Google Skills Boost student training-credits application form; $200 credits are issued to your account and usable on Google Skills courses/badges within 1 year.
  - *Expiry:* $200 Google Skills credits expire one year after issuance; free labs/Arcade have no stated end.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse with GCP usage credits — this buys courses/badges, not compute. A separate 'apply for free Google Cloud credits' line on the /edu hub for students appears to funnel to the same faculty/teaching-credit program (per the hub, students get free credits via the higher-ed program, i.e., via faculty).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Cloud for Education — research credits ('Academic Research Grants', up to $5,000)** (high) — Researchers can apply for up to $5,000 in GCP credits to advance research (computing power; GCP tools; proposal-based). Eligible roles: Faculty, Postdoctoral Researcher, PhD Student (and IT Admin option on the form), from accredited higher-education institutions or eligible nonprofit research institutions. Application requires: email (institution account preferred), country (restricted list of ~60 countries — program 'currently only available in' those countries), job title, organization (select from institution list), researcher/faculty directory link, project name, start date, a ≤250-word proposal (research problem, GCP tools, timeline, milestones, how GCP supports future work; Google Doc link allowed), field of research, intended use (proof of concept / evaluation-comparison / workshop-training / open-source development), optional Google Scholar link.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty / PhD students / postdocs at accredited higher-ed or eligible nonprofit research institutions in the listed approved countries; institutional email preferred; faculty directory link required. Related institutional program: GPAR (Google Public Sector — Program for Accelerated Research) with co-investment, industry-leading pricing for AI accelerators (GPUs/TPUs), early access to TPUs/foundation models/datasets (AlphaFold, Earth AI), agentic AI (Co-scientist, AlphaEvolve), compliance (HIPAA, CMMC 2.0, FedRAMP), on-campus research days — GPAR is currently US-only.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/ (linked from cloud.google.com/edu/researchers 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'). Submit the Academic Research Grant form with the fields above; credits are awarded to the institutional account and redeemable per the education-credits redemption guide.
  - *Expiry:* The public pages do not state a redemption window for research credits (teaching credits: 1 year post course-start; assume similar ~1-year usage window until confirmed in the FAQ); GPAR is a standing institutional program (US only).
  - *Gotchas:* Country-restricted list; institution must appear in the form's institution list ('begin typing and select'). Amount is 'up to $5,000' — awards may be less and are grant-reviewed. GPAR co-investment benefits are US-only. Institutional (not individual student) gate: undergraduates are not listed roles.
  - *Source:* https://edu.google.com/programs/credits/research/
- **Product-specific free trials for GCP databases/BI (no GCP-usage credit needed)** (high) — Separate short trials available on a GCP billing account: Spanner 90-day free trial instance (10 vCPUs? listed as '10 GB of storage' in docs: '10 vCPUs...' — docs say 90-day Spanner instance with 10GB storage at no cost); AlloyDB for PostgreSQL 30-day trial cluster (8 vCPU basic + 1TB storage capacity); Looker 30-day trial (all standard features); Cloud SQL 30-day trial. These require a billing account but cost nothing for the trial window.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GCP billing account (Free Trial or Paid); one instance per product per the trial terms.
  - *How to claim:* In GCP console, provision the trial instance (Spanner/AlloyDB/Looker/Cloud SQL trial buttons in each product's UI) on a billing account; delete before trial end to avoid charges.
  - *Expiry:* 90 days (Spanner) / 30 days (AlloyDB, Looker, Cloud SQL); auto-conversion to billable instance afterward.
  - *Gotchas:* These are time-boxed, not free-forever; after trial end instances bill at standard rates unless deleted; GCP docs also point to Google Maps Platform free usage caps, Gemini AI Studio free tier, and Firebase no-cost plans as adjacent free offers.
  - *Source:* https://docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features
- **Google Cloud free trial: $300 credit for new customers** (high) — New GCP customers get $300 in free credit to try products (incl. Cloud SQL, BigQuery, AlloyDB, Spanner) and build a proof of concept. Per the live /free page: 'You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account.' The $300 is not charged against the always-free allotments. No day limit is printed on the current page (historically a 60-day trial; verify in console at claim). Credits apply to PayG Google-owned 1P GCP services; excludes Marketplace/third-party products.
  - *Eligibility:* New Google Cloud customers (first-time). Credit card / payment method IS required to hold a billing account - the faculty education page explicitly warns students that the Free Trial 'requires them to use a credit card.' No .edu requirement.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.google.com/free or console (billing free-trial flow), accept terms with a payment method; $300 credit attaches to the new billing account on signup. Product trials (Cloud SQL/Spanner/AlloyDB/Bigtable/Looker/GKE) are claimed from the 'Explore more offers' section of the same /free page.
  - *Expiry:* No duration published on today's page; historical term was 60 days. Unused credit lapses when the trial term ends; account converts to pay-as-you-go only on activation of the full paid account.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs free-tier confusion: $300 trial credit is finite; the 'Always Free' caps persist after the trial. Credit card required - not a no-card offer. Internet egress and any usage beyond free caps/credit is billed. Trial is one per person; students should prefer education credits (no card) over the trial.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **GCP Always Free tier (ongoing, ~24 products incl. BigQuery 1TB queries/month)** (high) — Live /free page lists: BigQuery 1 TB queries/month; Cloud Storage 5 GB-months Standard; GKE 1 Autopilot or Zonal cluster/month; Cloud Run functions 2M invocations/month; Firestore 1 GB storage; Pub/Sub 10 GB messages/month; Compute Engine 1 e2-micro instance/month; Datastream up to 100 GiB CDC from eligible first-party sources/month; Agent Engine first 180,000 vCPU-seconds + 360,000 GiB-seconds/month; Application Integration up to 400 executions + 20 GiB data/month; Artifact Registry 0.5 GB/month; Cloud Shell 5 GB disk; Secret Manager 6 secret versions/month; KMS Autokey 100 key versions + 10,000 ops/month; plus Vision/NLP/STT/Video quotas and Workflows/Cloud Build allotments. Page: 'There is no charge to use these products up to their specified free usage limit. The free usage limit does not expire, but is subject to change.'
  - *Eligibility:* All GCP customers, including students and startups, every month. No .edu email, no funding cap. A billing account (with payment method) still required to open a GCP project; $0 billed while inside caps.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim - free allotments are automatic for any new GCP billing account/project. Set budgets/alerts in Cloud Billing to cap overage.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual, resets monthly. No end date, but Google may change or remove allotments without notice ('subject to change').
  - *Gotchas:* SHRUNKEN free tier: legacy Cloud SQL micro-instance free hours, Memorystore free hours, Spanner free 20 vCPU-hr/32GB, and Dataflow 9,600 worker-hours are no longer listed - managed-DB/data-platform users should plan on the finite product trials + $300 instead. BigQuery's 10 GB free storage is no longer listed. Cloud Storage free space is 5 GB-months (vs the historical 50 GB). Overage beyond caps bills at standard rates. Egress to internet is not a free allotment.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program - 'Start' tier (up to $2,000 credits)** (high) — First rung of the revamped program. FAQ on the live page: 'Start ($2k) tier is designed for startups founded in the past 2 years, with a working MVP, a clear business model, and plans to seek venture funding soon.' The prototyping stage also includes instant access to Google AI Studio for rapid MVP prototyping. Adjacent benefits on the page: Gemini Startup Forum membership and equity-free accelerator cohorts.
  - *Eligibility:* Unfunded/pre-VC: founded within past 2 years, working MVP, clear business model, plans to seek VC; new Google Cloud customers founding digital-native products. Must be in a region where GCP is GA with self-service purchasing. Excludes IPO'd/acquired companies, educational institutions, government, nonprofits, personal blogs, dev shops, consultancies/agencies, crypto mining, token distribution contrary to regulation.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at cloud.google.com/startup/apply (Google sign-in). Prepare the 18-character GCP billing account ID (credits are deposited there) and a business email that owns/admins the GCP account. Stealth founders get in via investor/VC/accelerator referral. Automated responses: rejection in 5-7 days; 10+ days if information is missing.
  - *Expiry:* Credits usable for the program's first-year window (per footnotes, Year-1 coverage rules apply; funded tiers run two years). Re-application is allowed if prior credits were unused and in-year eligibility still met.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are non-transferable, cannot be shared/split/resold (violation = termination); cover only first-party PayG GCP services - subscription/prepay/annual-license billing and all Marketplace/Model Garden third-party products excluded; third-party models billed directly; acceptance at Google's sole discretion based on company age, stage, prior GCP credit usage, and funding type.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startups
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program - 'Scale'/'Scale AI' funded tiers (up to $200K-$350K over two years)** (high) — Headline: up to $350,000 in GCP credits over two years (AI startups), up to $200,000 (other startups), i.e. the program's 'Funded tiers (Scale, and Scale AI) range from $200K-$350k in credits'. Footnote math: Scale AI - Year 1: 100% of eligible spend up to $250,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000. Other startups - Year 1: up to $100,000; Year 2: 20% up to an additional $100,000.
