The Cloud SQL, Spanner, AlloyDB and Bigtable entries also say new customers get $300 credit on signup.
  - *Eligibility:* Set per product on each trial page; not verified one by one. No .edu requirement. Startup age and funding rules do not apply.
  - *How to claim:* From cloud.google.com/free, open the product's trial link ('Explore more offers') and create the trial instance in the console.
  - *Expiry:* Trial instance periods are 10 days (Bigtable), 30 days (Cloud SQL, AlloyDB, Looker, Gemini Enterprise Plus) or 90 days (GKE, Spanner).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial instances end when their period ends; check each product page for what happens next. Card requirements and region limits for each trial were not checked individually. Trial instances are separate from Free Tier usage.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/free
- **Students: 200 Google Skills training credits** (high) — Currently enrolled college and university students can request 200 Google Skills credits for hands-on labs, skill badges and courses on Google Skills, the platform formerly called Google Cloud Skills Boost (cloud.google.com/edu/students). Credits expire one year after they are issued. The same page lists Google Skills Arcade (no-cost gamified labs) and a no-cost YouTube preview of Google Cloud Certificates.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified college or university students at regionally accredited institutions in the listed countries. The list is long and includes the US, UK, Canada, Germany, India, Japan, Brazil, Mexico, Australia and Singapore. For-profit institutions are not supported (support.google.com/google-cloud-higher-ed/answer/10322652). The help text does not spell out a .edu rule or the verification method. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Submit the Google Skills training-credits application form (services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudskillsbooststudenttrainingcreditsapplication/) with your institutional details. Requests can take up to two weeks. For more credits, use the same form. Then use the credits on skills.google.
  - *Expiry:* One year after issue.
  - *Gotchas:* These are lab and training credits for Google Skills. The official pages do not describe them as Cloud Billing dollars, so they do not fund your own Cloud projects. The portal domain changed to skills.google in October 2025 (see sunset_notes).
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/students
- **Google Skills no-cost Starter subscription: 35 credits a month** (medium) — The Starter subscription costs nothing and gives learners 35 credits each month for hands-on labs in Google Skills (support.google.com/qwiklabs/answer/9139481). Google's Skills announcements and the Developer Program FAQ also cite 35 monthly credits for GEAR program participants and Google Cloud Innovators community members. Paid Monthly Pro and Annual Developer Premium plans sit above it.
  - *Eligibility:* Any learner with a Google account. Not limited to students, and no .edu rule. Innovators membership is joined through My Communities and Programs on the developer profile. The card requirement for the no-cost plan was not verified.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at skills.google, open the profile menu, go to Settings, then Subscriptions, and select the Starter (no cost) plan. For the Innovators or GEAR route, join through the program pages.
  - *Expiry:* Monthly allotment. Whether unused credits roll over was not verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits are for labs only. The exact reset and rollover rules, and whether the free plan asks for a card, were not checked directly.
  - *Source:* https://support.google.com/qwiklabs/answer/9139481?hl=en
- **Google Career Launchpad: no-cost AI and cloud training for higher education** (medium) — Career Launchpad gives higher-education institutions (and government and nonprofit workforce organizations) no-cost generative-AI and cloud training. It includes DeepMind's AI Research Foundations curriculum, Google Cloud Certificates, hands-on labs and Educator Guides. Faculty also get Google Skills credits to add labs to coursework. Google Skills Arcade is always-on and no-cost. Google Cloud Certificates carry an ACE recommendation of up to 10 college credits (page footnote). Career Dreamer is US-only. The page mentions a 50% certification discount for some learning-path completers; conditions were not checked.
  - *Eligibility:* Institutional program run by faculty facilitators at higher-education institutions. The page sets no .edu rule. Career Dreamer is US-only. The country list was not checked. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty apply through the Apply button on cloud.google.com/edu/faculty/career-launchpad (faculty form: services.google.com/fb/forms/googlecloudcareerlaunchpadapplication/).
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the page. Expiry for the Skills credits within this program was not confirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* This is non-cash training, not a credit balance. The certification discount and ACE college-credit conditions were not verified.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty/career-launchpad
- **Education (teaching) credits: up to $100 per teaching staff member and $50 per student** (high) — Eligible faculty can apply for up to $100 in Google Cloud credits per teaching staff member and up to $50 in Cloud credits per student (cloud.google.com/edu/faculty). Approval issues coupons: faculty get codes for themselves and staff, plus a URL to send to students. Each course's credit is tracked in its own Cloud Billing account. Credits cover most Google Cloud products, and free-tier usage does not count against them (Cloud Billing edu-grants doc). Credits may not be used for course infrastructure, commercial use or personal use.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty only; students cannot apply but can ask faculty to apply. Faculty must use an official school email on a valid .edu domain (or nominate another email if they have none). In the US the institution must be a regionally accredited program; outside the US it must be a higher-learning institution that awards degrees in an eligible country. For-profit institutions are excluded. The application form has a country list (includes the US, UK, Canada, India, Japan, Germany, Australia and Brazil). Up to 3 courses per submission, with course start no later than one year away. Students must confirm a school email to redeem. The support page says sign-up needs no credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/teaching (up to 3 courses, expected student and TA counts, school faculty-directory link). After approval, faculty get coupon codes and a student URL. Students verify their school email, then click Redeem now in the coupon email and accept the terms in the Google Cloud console. Questions go to CloudEduGrants@google.com.
  - *Expiry:* Redeemable from approval until 16 weeks after course start. Validity sources conflict: up to 1 year after course start (program page), 12 months after course start (help page), and 1 year after redemption (Cloud Billing docs). Confirm against the approval email.
  - *Gotchas:* The official amounts are per teaching staff ($100) and per student ($50). The '$100 per course' wording in the brief is not how the official page states it. Credits are non-transferable and tied to the school email. Google may revoke awards or change criteria at any time. Check spam for the confirmation email, which should arrive within 24 hours.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/faculty
- **Research credits: up to $5,000 for faculty, PhD students and postdocs** (medium) — Researchers can apply for up to $5,000 in Google Cloud research credits (cloud.google.com/edu/researchers says 'Apply for $5,000 in research credits'; the application page text retrieved did not restate the figure). The credit must be activated within 60 days of the project start date on the application. It may be issued in increments and is for proof of concept, evaluation or comparison, workshops or training, and open-source development. The application asks for a funding plan for after the credit expires.
  - *Eligibility:* Faculty, PhD students and postdoctoral researchers at accredited higher-education institutions, plus eligible nonprofit research institutions, in approved countries (the list includes the US, UK, Canada, Germany, Japan, India, Australia, Singapore and South Africa). An institution account is preferred but not strictly .edu. PhD students may not use the credit for personal use. The GPAR program (Google Public Sector Program for Accelerated Research) is US-only and gives pricing co-investment, not credit. No card requirement is stated.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at edu.google.com/programs/credits/research. Provide personal and organization details, a project proposal (up to 250 words), the total from your pricing calculator with a link to it, a Google Cloud billing account ID, and a funding plan for after the credit expires.
  - *Expiry:* Expires 365 days after coupon redemption or when fully used, whichever comes first.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-transferable, with no sale or barter and no commercial use. The $5,000 headline comes from the researchers landing page. Confirm the cap on the form before relying on it, since 'up to' means awards can be smaller.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/edu/researchers
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program, Start tier: up to $2,000 in credits** (high) — The Start tier gives up to $2,000 in Google Cloud credits valid for one year (cloud.google.com/startup/benefits). Also: $200 in Google Skills training credits; 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free for new sign-ups (conditions apply); AI webinars and content; and Web3 partner perks for Web3 startups. This is the self-serve entry tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Technology startup with a working MVP, a clear business model and a well-defined product roadmap, that plans to seek venture funding soon. Founded within the last 24 months. Has not received Google Cloud credits beyond the free trial. The business email must match the company website domain, and the billing account must be owned and administered by someone on that domain. Not open to educational institutions, nonprofits, government entities, dev shops or consultancies, companies that have IPO'd or been acquired, or crypto mining. Google decides acceptance at its sole discretion. A Cloud Billing account is required; card requirement not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Create a Cloud Billing account in the console and note its 18-character billing account ID, since credits attach to it. Confirm a business email on your website domain. Apply through the Apply now button at cloud.google.com/startup. On approval, credits are issued to the billing account named in the application.
  - *Expiry:* Valid one year (official page). Some third-party sites say two years; the official page does not.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits apply only to Google first-party pay-as-you-go or consumption products. Subscription, prepay and annual-license products are excluded, as are third-party Marketplace and Model Garden offerings. Third-party models (such as Claude) are billed directly. Credits cannot be shared, split or resold. Workspace is free only if the company domain had no paid Workspace plan in the 31 days before applying. Re-applying is possible if earlier credits went unused.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Google for Startups Cloud Program, Scale tier: up to $200,000 (AI-first up to $350,000)** (high) — Scale tier: up to $200,000 in Google Cloud credits over two years. Year 1 covers 100% of spend up to $100,000; year 2 covers 20% of spend up to a further $100,000. AI-first startups: up to $350,000 (year 1 100% up to $250,000; year 2 20% up to $100,000 more). Also: 12 months of Google Workspace Business Plus free (separate application), $600 per month of Google Maps Platform credits (separate application), up to $12,000 in Google Cloud Enhanced Support credits for one year, Google Skills training credits (the benefits page lists $500 for this tier; verify), and a Startup Success Manager as you grow.
  - *Eligibility:* Venture-backed startups. Pre-Seed or Seed rounds within the last 5 years; if a Series A has been raised, it must be within the last 12 months. Founded within the last five years (benefits page; the program footnote says 3 to 5 years depending on tier). Has not received more than $5,000 in Google Cloud credits before. Non-venture funding (private equity, bank loans, government grants, crowdfunding, friends and family) does not qualify. Stealth startups are accepted on investor or accelerator verification. The AI-first tier requires Gemini or Gemma as a foundation of the primary product (AI program page). Must be in a region where Google Cloud is generally available and sold through self-service purchasing. Acceptance is at Google's discretion, weighing funding stage and type, business model, founder experience, product usage, age, growth and total funding. No .edu rule. A billing account is required; card requirement not stated.
  - *How to claim:* Have your funding round details, any investor or accelerator affiliation (enter these for stealth verification), billing account ID and website-domain business email ready. Apply at cloud.google.com/startup (Apply now). Credits are issued to the billing account after approval. Maps and Workspace benefits need separate applications.
  - *Expiry:* Credits run over two years in year-1 and year-2 tranches. The exact expiry of unused balances was not in the official text read.
  - *Gotchas:* Same first-party-only exclusions and third-party model billing as the Start tier. Credits cannot be shared, split or resold. Prior credits above $5,000 disqualify. Some third-party guides say the Scale tier is usually reached through a partner investor or accelerator; the official page does not say so. Approval is not guaranteed.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup/benefits
- **Startups in prototyping: free Gemini API access in Google AI Studio** (medium) — The prototyping track of the Google for Startups program gives free access to the Gemini API in Google AI Studio, with API key management, access to Google models such as Gemini, Gemini Omni and Nano Banana, and Build mode (cloud.google.com/startup and startup.google.com/cloud). This is product access, not a Cloud Billing credit.
  - *Eligibility:* Developers and builders at small businesses, bootstrapped startups and self-funded ideas, which are the stated criteria. No venture funding is required. No .edu rule. Card and regional requirements were not verified.
  - *How to claim:* Use the Start building in AI Studio link on cloud.google.com/startup and sign in with a Google account to create an API key.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated. Free-tier quotas can change.
  - *Gotchas:* Rate limits and data-use terms for the free tier were not verified in this run. Third-party models are not covered by program credits.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.google.com/startup
- **2025–26 change:** 2024-11-08 (AI Startups Summit, Bengaluru): Scale tier was restructured/raised — the prior program's 'up to $150,000 over 2 years' became $200,000 (or $350,000 for AI-first) with the Year-1 100% match / Year-2 20% structure; the pre-2024 tiered Grow/Scale legacy offers (which varied ~$5k-$150k by funding stage) were folded into the current Start ($2,000) / Scale ($200k/$350k) two-tier model — the earlier larger multi-stage tiers are gone. The standalone self-serve 'Google Cloud for Students' $300/12-month credit program is no longer advertised on current official pages (cloud.google.com/edu/students now lists faculty-applied course credits + free Google Skills access, 200 credits); it appears sunseted in favor of the faculty/course-based Education credits program (medium confidence — university IT pages still cite the old $300 offer). Historical note: GCP Free Trial was extended from 30 to 90 days back in Aug 2020 (unchanged since). Vertex AI's older '1M free GenAI units' promotional offer has not been re-announced in 2025-2026; AI free access now flows through the Gemini API Free tier and GCP Free Tier instead. 2026 changes worth flagging: Gemini API Free-tier rate limits moved to per-account dashboard (static per-model table removed from docs), unrestricted API keys blocked from June 19 2026, and paid tier now requires min $5 prepay.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes observed today (2026-10-22) on live pages: (1) The startup program is now branded "Google for Startups Cloud Program" at cloud.google.com/startups; the task's "known so far: up to $200,000" equals the current non-AI "Scale" funded-tier cap, but the headline cap is now up to $350,000 over two years ("Scale AI" tier: Year 1 100% coverage up to $250,000; Year 2 20% up to an additional $100,000; non-AI "Scale": Year 1 up to $100,000 + Year 2 up to $100,000). Legacy partner-accelerator network (Y Combinator/500/Techstars-era "Accelerate" listings) is replaced on the page by generic "equity-free accelerators" cohorts plus a "Gemini Startup Forum." Old URL cloud.google.com/programs/startups now 404s. (2) The Always-Free tier was restructured on cloud.google.com/free: the legacy free allotments for Cloud SQL (micro instance 750h/mo + 20GB SSD/375GB HDD), Memorystore, Spanner, and Dataflow (9,600h/mo) are no longer listed, and BigQuery no longer lists the 10GB storage free allotment (only 1TB/mo queries); Cloud Storage was cut to "5 GB-months Standard" and Cloud Functions was replaced by "Cloud Run functions." GCP now pairs this with finite product-specific free trials (30-90 days) for managed databases/data platforms. Confidence in this "changed" comparison is medium - I read today's page (high) but could not archive-compare (web search budget exhausted this session). (3) The $300 trial page no longer states a day limit or "60 days" wording; it says "You won't be charged until you activate your full paid account" (activation-based; historical 60-day term not visible today). (4) "Cloud Skills Boost" is rebranded as "Google Skills" (student offer is "200 free Google Skills credits"). (5) New 2025-2026 offers not previously on GCP: Gemini Enterprise Plus edition 30-day trial, Agent Engine free allotment (180,000 vCPU-s/360,000 GiB-s per month), Datastream free 100 GiB CDC/month, GKE 90-day trial. No GCP free offer appears to have been fully closed in this window - the major move is shrinkage of the always-free tier plus product trials and a bigger AI-leaning startup program. All findings above were verified by reading the cited official pages directly on 2026-10-22.
- **2025–26 change:** Corrections/sunsets vs. the 'known so far' baseline, all checked against official Google pages on 2026-10-22: (1) FREE TRIAL DURATION: the $300 credit is valid for 90 days, not 60 — the current docs (docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/free-cloud-features), signup FAQs, and the archived Jan-30-2025 Free Trial T&Cs all state 90 days/3 months; no 2025–2026 shortening found. (2) CLOUD SQL ALWAYS FREE: the old 'Cloud SQL Always Free plan' (free shared-core instance, 750 h/month, storage/backup/egress allowances in select regions) is NO LONGER on the Cloud SQL pricing page — the only official free Cloud SQL offer today is the 30-day free trial instance (Enterprise Plus, 8 vCPU/64 GB/100 GB) plus the $300 trial credit; treat the 750 h C2D claim as stale. (3) SPANNER FREE 96 GB NODE (us-west1): not present on the current Spanner pricing page; replaced by the 90-day free trial instance (10 GiB, Standard edition + Graph, regional-only) usable by both new and existing customers, with 30-day grace before data deletion. (4) BIGQUERY '100,000 slot-hours/month free': not published on the current BigQuery pricing page (which lists only 1 TiB free on-demand queries + 10 GiB free storage per month, plus free batch loads and first 2 TiB/mo of gRPC Storage Write API) — likely subsumed by the 2023–2024 move to Editions/slots pricing; treat the 100K slot-hour figure as superseded. (5) STARTUP PROGRAM REBRAND: 'Google Cloud Startups Accelerator' (cloud.google.com/startups, previously up to $150k single tier) is now 'Google for Startups Cloud Program' at cloud.google.com/startup — the old /startups URL serves the new program. Current structure: Start $2k tier (founded <2 years, MVP, pre-VC) + Scale (Y1 up to $100k + Y2 up to +$100k = $200k) + Scale AI (Y1 up to $250k + Y2 up to +$100k = $350k), with the $5,000-prior-credits gate and non-venture-funding exclusion for Scale tiers; marketing copy on cloud.google.com/free says 'up to $200,000 USD (up to $350,000 for AI startups)'. (6) EDUCATION: no general free GCP dollar credits for students; student offer is 200 Google Skills credits expiring after 1 year; the substantive education offers are faculty teaching credits ($100/faculty + $50/student) and GPAR research credits ($5,000, US-only). Note: no 2025–2026 sunset announcement changelogs were retrievable (web-search budget was exhausted mid-task), so items 2–4 'no longer listed' reflects absence from the live official pages today rather than a dated official announcement.
- **2025–26 change:** Legacy "Cloud Startups" program materially changed: the long-standing terms of "up to $200,000 in GCP credits, typically 12-month terms, mostly US-based startups" (the prior known baseline) no longer appear on the current program pages. As read today (2026-10-22), cloud.google.com/startup and startup.google.com/cloud both present the restructured "Google for Startups Cloud Program": up to $350,000 over two years, split into AI (Year-1 $250K) and non-AI (Year-1 $100K) tiers, eligibility "Pre-Seed to Series A, founded within the last 3-5 years, <$5,000 previously received GCP credits", with no US-entity requirement stated on the page. Exact 2025/2026 transition dates could not be news-verified today (this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted; the session user may raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION to allow that), so the "replacement" characterization is based on the live pages rather than a dated announcement — medium confidence on timing, high confidence on the current terms. Also current-state notes: (1) the $300/90-day trial and Always Free quotas are unchanged on today's official pages; (2) GKE's permanent free cluster is now phrased as "one free Autopilot or zonal Standard cluster per month" with compute/networking explicitly excluded; (3) GCP offers no free bare-metal or dedicated-server capacity at any tier — the only free "server" is the e2-micro micro-VM (3 US regions) and Cloud Run; (4) the Skills Boost domain no longer resolves — the education-facing free offering is now bundled under "Google Skills".
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22, no core offer was sunset: the $300/90-day trial, the Always-Free e2-micro VM tier, and the startup + education programs are all live and verified on official pages today. Verified structural notes (no web-searchable 2025-2026 change history available because this session's WebSearch budget was exhausted - conclusions below rest on page reads only, not on diffed changelogs): (1) The startup program now presents a THREE-tier structure - 'Start $2K' for unfunded startups founded in the last 2 years, plus funded 'Scale' and 'Scale AI' tiers ($200K-$350K) - a new/expanded format vs. the older single 'GCP for Startups' ($1M, founded-within-5-years, up-to-50-FTE) model that was widely documented pre-2025; old URLs cloud.google.com/programs/startup and cloud.google.com/boost now 404, and the current page is cloud.google.com/startup (cloud.google.com/gcp-startups still serves, 200). The Scale AI tier + 'Build with Google DeepMind' AI-credits framing (AI startups 100% to $250K Y1 / 20% to $100K Y2) reflects a post-2025 AI-first program redesign. (2) Education credits are now routed through edu.google.com forms (teaching credits, $5,000 research credits, 200 student training credits) and the 'cloud.edu free trial warning' PDF, a current setup. (3) GPAR (researcher fast-track) is flagged 'Currently only available in the United States'. If you need a 2025-2026 changelog (exact dates the startup tiers were reshaped or the trial terms edited), that requires a fresh WebSearch - this session's 200-call budget was consumed before the change-history could be verified; all amounts/durations above were read directly from the live official pages on the date of the report.
- **2025–26 change:** 1) Startup program renamed/restructured: the legacy 'Google Cloud Startup Program' (commonly cited as up to $200K/2-year funded track plus a YC-alumni track) is gone; cloud.google.com/startups/program and cloud.google.com/programs/startup-program now 404. The live offer (2026) is the 'Google for Startups Cloud Program': Start tier $2k; Scale/Scale AI $200K–$350K over 2 years, with AI startups capped at $250K Year 1 (100% match) + $100K Year 2 (20% match), other startups $100K Y1 + $100K Y2; new 12-month Google Workspace Business Plus benefit and monthly Google Maps Platform credits added. 2) Trial: current official terms are $300/90 days (not the 30/60-day figures seen in third-party summaries, including some of today's truncated page renders); a 30-day recovery grace period after expiry is now documented. 3) Trial restrictions are explicit and new-ish: GPUs/TPUs cannot be added on non-billable trial accounts; the $300 cannot pay Gemini API-in-AI-Studio or managed partner-model costs — so the 'GPU/ML-training' front on GCP trial means CPU (e2s) + free-tier e2-micro, with real GPU/TPU access requiring a paid account. 4) Docs domain moved: cloud.google.com/free/docs/* now 301-redirects to docs.cloud.google.com/free/docs/*. 5) Method caveat: WebSearch was unavailable for this run (shared session search budget exhausted), so 2025–2026 change-detection relied on the live official pages themselves; items I could not re-verify (Colab free quotas, Google Skills credit prices, per-product trial renewal rules) are marked medium/low confidence rather than carried from memory. 6) No evidence found this session that the always-free e2-micro or the education faculty/research grants were sunset — all confirmed live on 2026-10-22.
- **Critic correction** (Google Cloud Free Trial + always-free tier (Cloud SQL, BigQuery, Spanner)): Duration is wrong. The $300 Welcome credit is valid for 90 days from signup, not 60. Free-tier products do not draw down the credit. The trial billing account closes when the credit is spent or 90 days pass, whichever comes first.
- **Critic correction** (Google for Startups (Cloud)): Term length is wrong. Scale-tier credits are paid over two years: Year 1 is 100% of spend up to $100,000 (up to $250,000 for AI-first startups), and Year 2 is a 20% credit on spend up to an extra $100,000. The Start tier's $2,000 is valid for one year.
- **Critic correction** ($300 credit for 60 days (200+ products)): The $300 Free Trial credit is valid for 90 days, not 60. Google's free-tier page describes 20+ free products; '200+ products' is not stated.
- **Critic correction** (Up to ~$200,000 in GCP credits ... typically on 12-month terms): The $200,000 Scale-tier credit runs over 2 years: up to $100,000 in year 1 plus 20% (up to an additional $100,000) in year 2. The 12-month term applies to the $2,000 Start tier. AI startups can reach $350,000.

### IBM Startup with IBM

- **Startup with IBM credit program (Builder/Premium, up to $120K)** (high) — As of 2026-10-22 the program is no longer publicly available. https://developer.ibm.com/startups/ and its subpages (e.g. /startups/premium/) return 301 redirects to a generic 'IBM Build Partners Technical Group' community page (community.ibm.com), which contains no Startup-with-IBM content, no credit tiers, and no application form. The historical structure was Builder ~$1,000/mo + Premium ~$10,000/mo of IBM Cloud credits over 12 months. The task's claim that the current public offer is a $200 new-account credit plus always-free services is consistent with what the live pages show.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — program appears wound down; no application page exists as of 2026-10-22
  - *How to claim:* No longer claimable — application/program pages removed; URLs 301 to generic community group page
  - *Expiry:* Program no longer publicly listed as of 2026-10-22 (wind-down observed via redirect of all program subpages)
  - *Gotchas:* Do not confuse with other IBM partner programs; the community group it redirects to (IBM Build Partners Technical Group, 961 members, watsonx-tagged) is a general community, not a startup credit program
  - *Source:* https://developer.ibm.com/startups/
- **$200 IBM Cloud new-account credit (30 days)** (high) — New IBM Cloud accounts get USD 200 of general cloud credit usable for 30 days ('Try any IBM Cloud product with $200 in credit to use over the next 30 days'). The 30-day trial also includes 2 GB of runtime and container memory free for 30 days, access to provision up to 10 services, and free help-desk support. This is the current successor offer for startups/individuals now that the Startup program is gone.
  - *Eligibility:* New IBM Cloud accounts; credit card NOT required for the 30-day trial. Free-tier signup 'asks for your credit card to verify identity' but you are not charged for free-tier usage. No .edu or startup-age/funding requirements stated; no region limit stated on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at cloud.ibm.com/registration: 'Try IBM Cloud free for 30 days' or 'Get started for free'; credit applies to the account automatically; no promo code needed for the base $200
  - *Expiry:* Credit valid 30 days from account creation; trial features last 30 days; always-free products 'never expire'
  - *Gotchas:* Trial vs free: $200 credit and the 2 GB memory offer are time-boxed (30 days) and are a trial, not a free tier; always-free (Lite) products are the non-expiring part. Non-Lite usage beyond the credit is billed; CC requested for identity at free-tier signup even though trial needs none.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.ibm.com/registration
- **Always-free IBM Cloud 'Lite' services (40+ products)** (high) — 40+ products have an always-free 'Lite' plan with no credit card charge for Lite usage. Documented limits include: Hyper Protect Virtual Server 1 vCPU; IBM Cloudant 1 GB storage; Db2 SaaS 200 MB storage; Event Streams 1 partition; Watson Speech to Text 500 min/mo; Watson Text to Speech 10,000 chars/mo; Watson Language Translator 1,000,000 chars/mo; Watson Assistant 10,000 API calls/mo; API Connect 50,000 calls/mo; Container Registry 5 GB/mo pull; App ID 1,000 events/mo; Watson ML 20 capacity unit-hours; plus free 'Terraform-as-a-service' Schematics, and 30-day trials for Secrets Manager and IBM Cloud Internet Services.
  - *Eligibility:* Any IBM Cloud account (free-tier signup asks for a CC to verify identity, but 'you will not be charged for any usage within the free tier' and Lite products 'never expire and you can't be charged for them—ever'); no .edu or startup requirement
  - *How to claim:* Create a free IBM Cloud account, then provision services on the 'Lite' plan instead of paid plans; Lite is a plan choice at provisioning time, nothing to apply for
  - *Expiry:* Always-free, no expiry for Lite plans; monthly quotas reset on the 1st of each month
  - *Gotchas:* Lite instances sleep after 10 days of inactivity and are deleted after 30 days; hitting a monthly cap suspends that service for the rest of the month; egress/data-transfer and any non-Lite (paid) usage is billed normally — 'unlimited inbound' is not guaranteed and Lift CLI is the only page item stating unlimited inbound.
  - *Source:* https://www.ibm.com/cloud/free
- **Service-specific promo credits (new accounts)** (medium) — In addition to the base $200, the official free page lists promo credits for new accounts: USD 1,000 IBM Db2 Warehouse on Cloud; USD 1,000 for 120 days for Bare Metal Servers for VPC (code VPC1000); USD 2,500 for up to 90 days for IBM Power Virtual Server (code POWERVS2500); USD 5,000 for 90 days for Backup & Recovery (code BUYBRS); USD 500 over first 3 months via code TRYCOS; USD 400 for IBM Cloud Logs.
  - *Eligibility:* New-account/first-purchase offers; codes suggest claim-at-provisioning; no .edu requirement. Startup- or region-specific restrictions not stated on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Cited codes are entered/applied when ordering the corresponding service in a new IBM Cloud account (offer codes like VPC1000, POWERVS2500, BUYBRS, TRYCOS)
  - *Expiry:* Per-offer: 30 days (Db2 Warehouse), 120 days (VPC), 90 days (Power Virtual Server, B&R), 3 months (TRYCOS)
  - *Gotchas:* These are one-off first-use credits tied to specific services — they don't substitute for the always-free Lite plans; availability of the listed codes may vary by account/region and the page does not spell out redemption mechanics.
  - *Source:* https://www.ibm.com/cloud/free
- **IBM SkillsBuild (free education platform, student/faculty/K-12 tracks)** (high) — IBM's education offering is 100% free: skillsbuild.org describes '100% free learning' with courses in AI, cybersecurity, data, cloud, and industry tracks; audience tiers for adult learners, university students/educators (AI projects, 'portfolio-ready projects'), and high-school students/teachers (teacher toolkit, foundation skills). Includes industry-recognized digital credentials, an 'AI Level Up' assessment, and free virtual events. IBM's stated goal: skill 30 million people globally by 2030. This is the main 'education license/free-for-students' substitute that remains — it is free content, not cloud credits, and there is no .edu-gated license today.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone (no .edu email requirement stated, no CC required); university and high-school tracks are for students/educators but are publicly accessible
  - *How to claim:* Create a free account at skillsbuild.org (or via developer.ibm.com) and take courses/pathways; credentials issued on course completion
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — perpetual free access; IBM 'skill 30 million by 2030' commitment is the program horizon
  - *Gotchas:* It is learning content, not a cloud-credit offer — do not expect any IBM Cloud compute/storage from it; credential value is 'industry-recognized' but employer-dependent.
  - *Source:* https://skillsbuild.org
- **2025–26 change:** - Startup with IBM (historically up to ~$120,000 in IBM Cloud credits via Builder ~$1,000/mo and Premium ~$10,000/mo, 12 months) appears wound down by late 2026: developer.ibm.com/startups/ and /startups/premium/ now 301-redirect to a generic 'IBM Build Partners Technical Group' community page with no program content, tiers, or application path; ibm.com/cloud/startups resolves to a generic products listing with no startup program. The live successor offers are the $200 new-account credit (30 days), the 40+ always-free Lite services, and the service-specific promo credits listed above. Exact sunset date not published on the pages checked today. - No student-specific cloud-credit program (e.g., .edu-gated credits) is publicly listed today; the education free offer is SkillsBuild's 100% free learning platform. - IBM developer learning portal paths such as developer.ibm.com/learn and ibm.com/education now 404; free learning content has consolidated around developer.ibm.com (courses/tutorials/learning paths, all free) and skillsbuild.org.

### Microsoft Azure

- **Azure Free Account — $200 credit + 12 months of free services** (high) — $200 Azure credit usable within the first 30 days (forfeited if unused; not banked/transferable). Plus 12 months of free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services and 65+ 'always-free' services, including some AI/Foundry tools: Azure Speech in Foundry Tools (0.5M neural chars/mo, always), Content Safety in Foundry Control Plane (5,000 text records or images/mo, always), Document Intelligence in Foundry Tools (500 pages S0, 12 mo), Azure Vision (5,000 transactions S1/S2/S3, 12 mo), AI Search, Azure SQL DB (100,000 vCore-sec serverless/mo + 32GB), 750 h/mo of B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 VMs. No free LLM/OpenAI token tier. After 30 days or credit exhaustion you must move to pay-as-you-go to keep the 12-month free amounts; overage beyond free monthly amounts then bills P2P.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only (never had an Azure account / 12-mo free services). Individual or org. 12-month free services NOT available to customers signing up directly for P2P in China and India. Country/region is locked at sign-up and cannot be changed later.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account. 2) Choose 'Azure free account'. 3) Complete the 3-step account setup (profile, billing address, payment card). 4) A spending limit is on by default; you immediately get $200/30 days + 12-mo free amounts. 5) To keep free services after 30 days, remove the spending limit (convert to P2P) — only usage above the free amounts is then charged.
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit expires at the end of day 30. The 12-month free-service amounts end at 12 months (the 65+ 'always-free' services continue). Inactive accounts (>90 days) may have compute resources removed with 30 days' notice.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required at signup (temporary $1 authorization; non-prepaid card). It is a trial-then-pay offer, not a perpetual free tier. Services not on the free list (notably Azure OpenAI/LLM tokens, and egress) bill immediately. Unused $200 is lost. Free quantities don't roll over month to month.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit / 12 months, no credit card** (high) — $100 Azure credit valid for 12 months (usable on most Azure products, incl. Azure OpenAI after you apply for access), plus 25+ free services, free monthly amounts of 20+ popular + 65+ always-free services, and free software downloads (Visual Studio Community/Enterprise, SQL Server, Windows Server, R, Visio, Windows 10/11 VMs without needing a Windows 11 Enterprise license). Renewable every year while still a student — each successful renewal grants a fresh $100 and a new 12-month term. A no-credit-card 'Azure for Students Starter' variant also exists.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time students age 18+ at an accredited 2- or 4-year degree-granting institution. Must verify academic status via school/institution email (.edu or org domain) or via GitHub Student Developer Pack. Not available for MOOCs or for-profit professional training. One subscription per eligible customer, non-transferable, can't be combined with other offers.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/free/students. 2) Sign up with your school email (or GitHub Student Pack) to verify student status — no credit card. 3) Receive the $100 credit + 25+ services + software downloads. 4) To use Azure OpenAI/LLM, separately apply for access in the AI Foundry portal. 5) Renew annually by re-verifying status (reminder email ~30 days before the anniversary).
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months; renewable yearly while eligible (a new $100 + new 12-month term per renewal). If you exhaust the credit before 12 months the subscription disables until you upgrade to P2P. Deployed resources are decommissioned at term end; you have a 90-day window to upgrade to P2P to keep them.
  - *Gotchas:* No credit card required, but continuing after the credit means pay-as-you-go. There is no dedicated free LLM tier — OpenAI/LLM usage is application-based and consumes your $100 credit. Cannot be combined with other offers. Inactivity >90 days may deprovision compute. Remaining credit is tracked in the Azure Sponsorships portal.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Azure for Startups (self-serve, no investor code) — $1,000 instantly, up to $5,000 total** (high) — For new-Azure-customer startups without an investor referral: $1,000 in Azure credits instantly, no application; plus up to $4,000 more after business verification = up to $5,000 total. Initial $1,000 credits valid 90 days; post-verification credits valid 180 days. Access to the full Azure catalog up to credit limits. This self-serve track can later grow into the Microsoft for Startups / Founders Hub program (up to $150,000) as you demonstrate adoption.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers / early-stage B2B startups; privately held, for-profit; HQ in a country where Azure is available; not an educational institution, government, or consultancy; no cryptocurrency mining; must use a personal consumer Microsoft account (work/school email rejected). Credit card required for identity verification and for any usage beyond the credits.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at startups.microsoft.com ('Get started now') or the Azure for Startups signup on azure.microsoft.com/free/students footer. 2) Choose 'Continue without a code' / 'Build your startup', verify (e.g., via LinkedIn), then complete the 3-step Azure account setup (profile, address, payment card) → $1,000/90 days active. 3) Complete business verification to unlock up to +$4,000 (180 days). 4) If you later obtain an investor referral code, apply for the investor track.
  - *Expiry:* Self-serve: $1,000 = 90 days; +$4,000 = 180 days (post-verification). Credits auto-convert to pay-as-you-go when used or expired. (When on the Founders-Hub track: activate credits within 90 days of accepting the program; once activated, valid up to 2 years, no extensions.)
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (identity verification + non-covered services + P2P transition). Overage beyond credits bills P2P. Country/region locked at signup. Framing discrepancy: the MfS 'application' page shows a '$200' no-code starter and '$1,000' for the code path, while the FAQ/self-serve offer states '$1,000 (90d) + up to $4,000 (180d)'; treat $1,000 as the current no-investor baseline. Credits are tied to the specific MSA/subscription and cannot be transferred.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/mfs-faqs
- **Microsoft for Startups / Founders Hub (investor-referred) — start ~$100,000, expand to up to $150,000** (high) — Startups with a 10-character referral code from a Microsoft Investor Network investor/accelerator/VC start with ~$100,000 in Azure credits and can unlock up to $150,000 total over time based on business verification, service adoption, and sustained Azure usage. The investor offer also bundles third-party benefits: GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, Microsoft 365 Business Premium, Visual Studio Enterprise, Dynamics 365, Power Platform, plus Azure Standard Support and a dedicated MfS point of contact. OpenAI/Foundry (LLM) usage is covered by the Azure credits. Program is open-ended on time; you reapply/requalify as you grow.
  - *Eligibility:* Pre-seed through Series B (NOT Series C or later). Privately held, for-profit B2B software/AI startup; working toward/at MVP. Must NOT have received more than $350,000 in lifetime free Azure credits. HQ in a country where Azure is available. Not an educational institution, government, consultancy, or agency; no crypto mining. Must use a personal consumer email (work/school/company-domain email rejected). Referral code comes from an Investor Network member (list not public).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get a 10-character investor referral code from your investor/accelerator/VC (Microsoft doesn't publish the list). 2) At startups.microsoft.com, 'Get started now', sign in with a personal Microsoft account, enter the code, verify contact email + phone (SMS). 3) Submit business info (registered name matching legal docs, industry, website, address). 4) 3-step Azure account setup with a payment card → $1,000 starter active while reviewed. 5) Review ~3 business days; on approval, credits expand up to $150,000. 6) Activate credits within 90 days of accepting the program agreement.
  - *Expiry:* Activate within 90 days of accepting the program agreement or the offer expires (reapply). Once activated, Azure credits valid up to 2 years; extensions are not available. Auto-convert to P2P when credits run out.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card required. Benefits (GitHub, M365, VS Enterprise, etc.) must be redeemed on the SAME personal Microsoft account and NOT on an existing enterprise account — 'Already redeemed' errors are common. Credits can't be transferred across accounts. Declined applicants may reapply after 14 days. The $350,000 lifetime free-Azure-credit cap and the Series-C ceiling are hard disqualifiers. Country/region locked at signup.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview
- **Azure OpenAI / Microsoft Foundry (Azure AI Foundry) — no free LLM tier; paid + application-based** (high) — There is NO free monthly tier or free API-call allowance for OpenAI/LLM models on Azure (now branded 'Azure AI Foundry' / 'Microsoft Foundry', with sub-brands Foundry Tools, Foundry Control Plane, Foundry IQ). Pricing is pay-as-you-go per input/output token, or provisioned throughput units (PTUs) with monthly/annual reservations; the Batch API returns completions within 24h at ~50% discount. The only 'free' way to get LLM tokens is to burn the credits from the other offers above ($200 free account / $100 student / $1,000-$5,000 startup). Other Foundry tools DO have free allowances (Speech 0.5M chars/mo always-free; Content Safety 5,000/mo; Document Intelligence 500 pages/12mo; Vision 5,000/12mo).
  - *Eligibility:* Requires an Azure account plus a payment method; model deployments are application-based (apply for access, specify use case/responsible-AI mitigations; historically gated by demand). Available only in selected regions per model.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Hold one of the credit offers above (or P2P). 2) In the Azure portal, create an Azure AI Foundry resource. 3) Request access to a model (e.g., GPT) by submitting a use case. 4) Deploy the model; token usage draws down your free credits first, then bills P2P.
  - *Gotchas:* Not a genuine free LLM tier — 'free' only via borrowed credits; once credits exhaust it is pay-as-you-go. Per-model/per-region deployment quotas and limits apply; high-demand models may be gated. Data egress and extra meters add cost. Rebrand means 'Azure OpenAI' docs/prices now live under 'Microsoft Foundry'.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/cognitive-services/openai-service/
- **Student developer tools — Visual Studio (Community/Enterprise), GitHub Student Pack, Azure Dev/Test credits** (medium) — Azure for Students includes free software downloads (Visual Studio Community/Enterprise, SQL Server Developer/Standard, Windows Server, R, Visio Professional, Windows 10/11 VMs). Eligible students get Visual Studio Enterprise with ~$150/month of Azure Dev/Test credits (renewable; no payment method needed — usage auto-stops when the monthly credit is consumed); Visual Studio Professional gives ~$50/month. The GitHub Student Developer Pack (free, .edu) bundles GitHub Copilot, VS Code tools, Databricks and more. 'Dev Tools for Teaching' extends software access to faculty/students of enrolled institutions.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified students (.edu or GitHub Student Pack). Visual Studio Enterprise for students is available through eligible programs (e.g., Student Ambassadors); the Dev/Test credits are strictly for development/test (non-production). GitHub Student Pack requires a .edu email. Dev Tools for Teaching is institution-licensed.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify student status (.edu / GitHub Student Pack) via Azure for Students. 2) Download Visual Studio / dev tools from your Visual Studio subscription benefits portal. 3) Claim the GitHub Student Developer Pack at github.com/student (free, .edu). 4) If on an eligible VS plan, activate the individual Azure Dev/Test credit subscription (no payment method; usage stops at the monthly cap).
  - *Gotchas:* Dev/Test credits are void if used for production. Microsoft no longer sells new Visual Studio Enterprise/Professional ANNUAL cloud subscriptions (existing customers can renew; new users go to visualstudio.microsoft.com/vs/pricing). GitHub Pack items carry their own terms/caps (e.g., Copilot free tier for students). Monthly Dev/Test credit does not roll over.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/subscriptions/vs-azure-eligibility
- **Azure for Students (higher-ed): $100 Azure credit + free services, no credit card** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students and the official offer page (ms-azr-0170p). Students get $100 in Azure credits to use within 12 months plus: free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (new Azure customers only), free monthly amounts of 65+ always-free services, access to the full Azure service catalog up to free amounts + the $100 credit, and access to Azure OpenAI and Microsoft Foundry with 1,700+ foundation models (OpenAI o1/GPT-4 series, Hugging Face, Meta, Cohere, Phi SLMs). Includes free 'Software Download Benefits' via the Azure Education Hub: Visual Studio (Community/Code/for Mac, Agents for VS), SQL Server Developer & Standard, Windows Server, Visio Professional, R Client/Server, Hyper-V Remote Tools, System Center, Azure DevOps Server, SQL Server Mobile Report Publisher. Renew annually while a student: each renewal gives a fresh $100 + another 12-month term (confirmed by MS staff in a March 2026 Q&A and the official FAQ; reminder emails ~30 days before expiry; no hard cap on renewals).
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time students at an accredited degree-granting 2- or 4-year university; affirm age 18+; verify academic status via your organization's (school) email address — Shibboleth where supported, or GitHub Student Developer Pack verification code as fallback; NOT available for MOOCs or for-profit professional training; one subscription per person; 'limited supply of activations, while supplies last'; not available in sovereign-cloud (US Gov, China, Germany) regions; offer available where Azure is commercially available.
  - *How to claim:* Go to azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students → sign up with a Microsoft or GitHub account → verify student status with school email (or GitHub code at signup.azure.com/studentverification?offerType=1) → accept offer terms; no credit card at sign-up. Track remaining credit in the Azure Sponsorships portal. After 12 months, re-sign up for the offer to renew; to renew you re-verify status.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit and 12-month free services window run from sign-up; renew annually while eligible. If credit is exhausted early the subscription is DISABLED (you can upgrade to Pay-As-You-Go via support; deployed resources are then decommissioned at the end of the 12-month term — 90 days to convert). Invoicing statements may appear monthly but are covered by the credit.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits cannot be used on Azure Marketplace offers and don't carry over between months or subscriptions; unused credit is forfeited at 12 months; if you previously held any Azure free trial/student offer on the same Microsoft identity you cannot sign up again (you'll hit a 'no access' wall and must convert that old sub to PAYG); the offer can be terminated at Microsoft's discretion; the 20+ free-for-12-months services apply only to NEW Azure customers; anything beyond free amounts deducts from the $100; the 'Visual Studio Pro for 12 months' item in many older guides is NOT in this offer anymore — Pro only comes from the separate GitHub Student Developer Pack; keep at least one resource/portal activity so the sub doesn't lapse as inactive (MS community advises 90-day activity, not official).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students(fetched2026-10-22;offerterms:https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0170p;FAQ:https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/faq)
- **Azure for Students Starter (K-12 path): free core-service tiers, no credit card, $0 spend limit** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 (official offer page ms-azr-0144p + Learn doc 'About the Azure for Students Starter program'). A second, lighter Azure student path — the same free/students page says 'Try Azure for Students or Azure for Students Starter — no credit card required.' Gives a verified K-12 (secondary/high-school) student free-tier access with a $0 spending limit to: Azure App Service, Azure Functions, Azure Notification Hubs, Azure Database for MySQL (in-app), Application Insights (Azure Monitor), and Azure DevOps. No commitment or time limit stated on the offer page; one subscription per eligible customer; not transferable; can't be combined with other offers; not for MOOCs/for-profit training; upgrade to Pay-As-You-Go at any time for the full catalog. (A Russian-localized Learn copy says the sub lasts a year and must be renewed — the en-US offer page governs: no time limit.)
  - *Eligibility:* Verified K-12 students; affirm age 13+ if residing in the US, 16+ if residing elsewhere; verify academic status through your organization's email (or Shibboleth where supported); no credit card.
  - *How to claim:* Enroll at the Azure for Students Starter offer page (azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0144p, linked from the 'Choose the right Azure account' section of azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students) → verify with school email/Shibboleth → account starts with a $0 spend limit; upgrade in the Azure portal to PAYG when you need more.
  - *Expiry:* Offer page: no commitment or time limit. Upgrade path: Pay-As-You-Go anytime.
  - *Gotchas:* $0 spending limit means you can ONLY use the free tiers of the listed services — everything else is blocked, not billed; limited to six service families (no VMs, no OpenAI models, no credits); one per customer; prior Azure trial on the same identity blocks signup.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0144p(plushttps://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/education-hub/azure-dev-tools-teaching/azure-students-starter-program)
- **Microsoft for Startups, self-service track: $1,000 Azure credits instantly (90 days), up to $5,000 (180 days) after business verification** (high) — Materially changed in 2025-2026. As of 2026-10-22, the 'Azure for Startups' card on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students (fetched today) states: $1,000 in Azure credits instantly, no application required; unlock up to $5,000 in total credits with business verification; credits valid 90 days (initial) and 180 days (post-verification); full Azure catalog up to credit limits; 'spending protection — credit card required only for identity verification and services beyond credit'; no upfront commitment, cancel anytime, moves to pay-as-you-go after credits expire. This replaced the old Founders Hub on July 1, 2025. As of June 2026 (official MS blog + Learn overview, updated 2026-08-05): the program is now single-path — apply at startups.microsoft.com, start building immediately with Startup credits while eligibility is verified, and 'unlock up to $150K in Startup credits over time' based on verified progress, service adoption, and sustained Azure usage; applications typically reviewed in 3 business days; LLM/AI access is via Microsoft Foundry (GPT-4-class models, 1,700+ foundation models).
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only; privately held, for-profit company developing a software-based product it owns; headquartered in a country where Azure is available; NOT an educational institution/government/consultancy/agency; no cryptocurrency mining; NOT raised Series C funding or later; NOT previously received more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits (updated cap; the legacy Founders Hub signup page still shows a $10,000 cap — the current Learn doc governs). A credit card is required (unlike the student offers) — only for identity verification and for any services/usage beyond the credits.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at startups.microsoft.com ('Get started') — the $1,000 is granted instantly with no application/verification wait; complete the business-verification checklist to unlock the remaining $4,000 (credits then valid 180 days); enter an Investor Network referral code during application if you have one. Manage/activate credits and benefits in the Azure Portal. After credits expire you move to pay-as-you-go; cancel anytime.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 valid 90 days from sign-up; $5,000 tier valid 180 days from verification. No renewals; expiration converts the sub to PAYG. June 2026 change: further credits up to $150K are unlocked incrementally 'over time' based on service adoption/sustained usage (not a fixed grant).
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required here (identity verification) — unlike the student offers; no dedicated technical support on the self-service track (that's the Investor-Network benefit); the 90/180-day windows are short — if you can't burn $5k in 6 months the account flips to PAYG; Microsoft can change/terminate the offer (as it did on 2025-07-01 with little notice); legacy Founders-Hub 'levels' (Expert/Champion Networks) no longer exist.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students(fetched2026-10-22);programoverview:https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview;changeannouncement:https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/blog/build-fast-scale-smart-and-sell-more-microsoft-for-startups-evolves-to-better-serve-the-startup-ecosystem/
- **Microsoft for Startups, Investor Network track: referral-gated, $100,000+ Azure credits + dedicated Microsoft point of contact** (medium) — Per the July 1, 2025 restructuring (The Register/TechRadar coverage + official blog) and the June 2, 2026 official blog: startups referred by a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partner (a select group of VCs, accelerators, and universities) enter a 'higher touch' track that 'starts with $100,000 in Azure credits' (press figures; the official Learn overview describes it less precisely as 'higher-value benefits earlier', 'additional Startup credits, expanded support, and third-party benefits'). Includes dedicated Microsoft POC, deeper Microsoft Marketplace and co-sell support, and invitation-only Pegasus program (dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) which Microsoft explicitly kept unchanged. The old Founders Hub tiers up to $150K and the Expert/Champion Networks were retired; existing activated legacy credits stayed valid until their expiry, then auto-converted to PAYG.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup must be tied to an Investor Network-affiliated VC/accelerator/university that issues you a referral code, entered during the startups.microsoft.com application; same company-level eligibility as the self-service track (private, for-profit, software product, no Series C+, HQ in an Azure region).
  - *How to claim:* Get the referral code from your investor/accelerator partner → apply at startups.microsoft.com and enter the code → you are routed to the enhanced track instead of the $1,000/$5,000 self-serve ramp.
  - *Expiry:* Credit validity follows program terms (legacy $100K-tier grants were ~180-day windows per level; new grants track the 'credits over time' model). Pegasus is invitation-only and ongoing.
  - *Gotchas:* The exact $100,000 figure is from press, not the current official Learn page (which I fetched today) — treat as 'about $100K+'; no funding-amount cap like AWS Activate, but the Series C+ and $350K lifetime-free-credit caps disqualify most well-funded companies; if your legacy Founders Hub credits expired without investor affiliation, your sub already converted to PAYG.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/blog/build-fast-scale-smart-and-sell-more-microsoft-for-startups-evolves-to-better-serve-the-startup-ecosystem/;https://www.theregister.com/off-prem/2025/07/01/microsoft-changes-conditions-for-azure-startup-credits/101398;https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview
- **Azure Free account (also open to students without .edu verification): $200 credit for 30 days + 12-month and always-free services** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/. For any new Azure customer (incl. students who don't have a qualifying school email): $200 credit usable within 30 days; free amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (750 h/mo B1s + B2pts v2 Arm + B2ats v2 AMD VMs; Azure SQL serverless 100,000 vCore-sec/mo + 32 GB; PostgreSQL & MySQL Flexible 750 h/mo B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB; Container Registry Standard 100 GB; SQL Managed Instance 720 vCore-h + 64 GB data + 64 GB backup; Vision 5,000 txns/tier) plus 65+ always-free services (DevOps 5 users, Functions 1M requests, App Service 10 apps/1 GB/1 h-day, Static Web Apps 100 GB bandwidth, Cosmos/DocumentDB 32 GB, IoT Hub 8,000 msg/day, Logic Apps 4,000 actions, Notification Hubs 1M, AI Search 50 MB/10k docs/3 indexes, Document Intelligence 500 pages S0, Translator 2M chars, Content Safety 5,000 records, Speech 0.5M neural chars/mo, Container Apps 180k vCPU-s/360k GiB-s/2M reqs, API Mgmt 1M calls/mo, Azure Maps 1,000–5,000 txns, AKS management free). LLM/AI: Azure OpenAI and Microsoft Foundry models accessible with the $200 (or student $100); the OpenAI 'free trial' token allowances (historically ~1,000 gpt-4o-mini tokens/day, 40k/mo, ~3 months; 50/day gpt-4o, o1, gpt-4; 500/day gpt-3.5-turbo) could not be re-verified today because the pricing tables render via JS and returned placeholders — treat exact token figures as unconfirmed. One free-credit account per person; not available in US Gov/China/Germany sovereign clouds.
  - *Eligibility:* Any NEW Azure customer (students, startups, individuals). Requires a phone number + a credit/debit card (non-prepaid) — card used for identity verification with a small reversed authorization hold; in Hong Kong and Brazil only credit cards are accepted; billing country must be in the supported list (~140 countries/regions). A startup on this path uses the $200 trial PLUS the $1,000→$5,000 Startup credits on a separate offer.
  - *How to claim:* azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/ → 'Try Azure for free' → sign in with Microsoft or GitHub account → enter phone + card → $200 credit and free tiers activate immediately. After 30 days, if you don't convert to PAYG, the account is disabled (unused credit is kept only if you convert early).
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit: 30 days. 12-month free amounts: 12 months from sign-up, then billed at PAYG. Always-free services: ongoing, no expiry. No renewals.
  - *Gotchas:* This is the one Azure offer that REQUIRES a credit card — students without .edu can't use it card-free; data egress (internet outbound), inbound-to-public-IP traffic, and any meter beyond the listed free amounts are charged against the $200 and then PAYG; '12-month free' services that are 'always free' are different products — check the Free/Are-You-Aware tabs on each pricing page; sovereign-cloud regions excluded; one per person.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/(fetched2026-10-22)
- **LLM & AI free tiers inside Azure (Microsoft Foundry / Azure AI services) — what students and everyone get at no cost** (medium) — From the official student/free pages fetched 2026-10-22: always-free (all customers, no CC): Azure Speech in Foundry Tools 0.5M neural characters/month; Azure AI Search 50 MB storage / 10,000 docs / 3 indexes per service; Azure Document Intelligence 500 pages S0 tier; Translator 2M characters; Content Safety 5,000 text records or images; Machine Learning 'Free' (no meter); Azure Maps 1,000–5,000 transactions; IoT 8,000 msgs/day. 12-month (new customers): Azure Vision 5,000 transactions each S1/S2/S3. Phi small language models) via Microsoft Foundry, billed to their $100 student credit or free quotas; the 'try Azure OpenAI' free-trial token quotas (e.g., gpt-4o-mini 1,000 tokens/day / 40,000/mo for 3 months; 50/day for gpt-4o, o1, gpt-4; 500/day gpt-3.5-turbo) are the long-standing published numbers but I could not re-confirm today (JS-rendered pricing tables returned '$–' placeholders), so treat per-model token quotas as possibly stale. Notably the 2026 pages rebrand Azure AI services as 'Microsoft Foundry'/'Foundry Tools'.

Azure OpenAI models on the student offer: valid Azure for Students subscription (no CC) with access requests/approval per model — access to some frontier models still requires an OpenAI access application stating use case.

- **Managed databases & data-platform free tiers (usable by students; the 'free tier' front)** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/. Managed-database offers: (always free, all customers) Azure SQL Database serverless 100,000 vCore-seconds/month + 32 GB storage; Azure Cosmos DB dedicated 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB/month free for 12 months then continues as 'Free' tier with 2 free GB storage + 500 RU/s for all customers; DocumentDB dedicated MongoDB cluster 32 GB; Database Migration Service free Standard compute; Azure Data Catalog unlimited users; Data Factory 5 low-frequency activities; Data Explorer free; Query Store free. (12 months, new customers) Azure Database for PostgreSQL Flexible Server 750 h B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; Azure Database for MySQL Flexible Server same; SQL Managed Instance 720 vCore-hours (4 or 8 vCore General Purpose) + 64 GB data + 64 GB backup; Azure SQL Database 750 h B5m/baseline + 100 vCore-min burstable. Free backup: 7 days for SQL DB/Managed Instance. These are the main free managed-database options; no CC needed for the student offer that bundles them.

  - *Expiry:* Always-free: ongoing (Cosmos free tier is a permanent tier, not 12-month). 12-month amounts: end at 12 months then PAYG. Post-12-month Cosmos DB falls to the permanent free 500 RU/s + 2 GB tier, not deletion.

- **Microsoft for Startups — Azure Startup Credits (milestone program, up to $150K)** (high) — Tiered 'Startup credits' on a sponsored Azure subscription: M1 $200 (sign up + verify with a personal LinkedIn account), M2 $5,000 (business verification: registered legal entity), M3 $25,000 (5+ Azure workloads active ~60 days; also grants a one-time 2-year extension to use credits), M4 $50,000 (7+ workloads ~60 days), M5 $150,000 (10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month Azure usage ~60 days). The 60-day clock starts once each workload has at least $1 of spend; all required workloads must stay above $1/day for the full 60 days. Tiers are NOT sequential — accounts are auto-evaluated and upgraded, so a scaling startup can jump tiers. Credits apply to eligible Azure consumption, select first-party Microsoft Foundry models, and GitHub Copilot/Codespaces/Actions (only if the Azure subscription is connected as the GitHub billing method). When credits are exhausted or expire, the subscription auto-converts to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Eligibility:* B2B software/AI/tech startups, pre-seed through Series B (must NOT have raised Series C or later); privately held, for-profit, not a subsidiary; owns its software product; HQ in a country/region where the program operates (country/region is locked at sign-up and cannot be changed; unsupported country = offer unavailable); not an educational institution, government, consultancy, or agency; not involved in crypto mining; company has NOT received more than $350,000 in lifetime free Azure credits. Sign-in must use a personal consumer Microsoft account — work/school/company-domain emails are rejected.
  - *How to claim:* 1) microsoft.com/en-us/startups → 'Get started now'; 2) sign in with a personal Microsoft account; 3) 'Continue without a code' (or enter a 10-char investor code, see separate finding); 4) verify identity with a personal LinkedIn account; 5) complete 3-step Azure account creation (profile, billing address, payment card); 6) 'Build your startup' dashboard shows $200 unlocked immediately (balance can lag up to 24h); 7) application reviewed in ~3 business days, decision by email; manage credits/portal in Azure Portal and MfS portal.
  - *Expiry:* M3 ($25K) grants a one-time 2-year extension of credit validity; otherwise no fixed validity per tier is published on the pages fetched — when credits are fully used or expired the subscription automatically becomes pay-as-you-go. Set Azure budgets/alerts; 'credits eventually run out' is Microsoft's own framing.
  - *Gotchas:* CREDIT CARD IS REQUIRED (non-optional: card number/expiry/CVV at sign-up; auto-converts to pay-as-you-go when credits run out or expire — no upfront commitment, subscription cancellable). Personal consumer email only. Country/region cannot be changed later. GitHub usage is NOT credit-covered out of the box — you must connect the Azure subscription as a GitHub payment method, and then only metered/eligible GitHub services draw on credits. Third-party (partner/community) Foundry models are separately billed and not covered. Egress and other metered Azure items are still billed through the credits, not free forever.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs
- **Microsoft for Startups Investor Network referral code ($1,000 starter credits, up to $200K cap, earlier tier unlocks)** (high) — Startups with a referral from a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partner (VC/accelerator) enter a 10-character code during application and immediately unlock $1,000 in starter Azure credits instead of $200 while their application is under review. Per the benefits page (updated 2026-06-15), eligible Investor Network-backed startups may unlock up to $200,000 in Startup credits (vs the $150K standard cap) plus expanded support and third-party benefits, and 'may unlock higher-value benefits earlier in their journey'. Microsoft does not publish the list of participating investors.
  - *Eligibility:* Same base program eligibility (pre-seed→Series B B2B software startup etc.), PLUS the referral code from your VC/accelerator. Code is obtained directly from your investor — not issued publicly. Business name/address must match legal documents or business verification fails and acceptance is delayed.

  - *Expiry:* Email verification code expires in 10 minutes. Credit/term rules follow the standard milestone program (2-year extension at the $25K milestone; auto pay-as-you-go on exhaustion).
  - *Gotchas:* Code is not public and investor lists are not shared — ask your accelerator/VC directly. Mismatched legal name/address = failed business verification. The $1,000 is while the application is under review; higher tiers follow the same workload/usage milestones (M3–M5) as the standard track.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/application
- **Microsoft for Startups — free software/tool benefits (M365, GitHub Enterprise, Power BI, Dynamics, LinkedIn, GPU, support)** (high) — Technical benefits table (learn.microsoft.com, updated 2026-08-12): Microsoft 365 Business Premium 50 seats/1 year; GitHub Enterprise 20 seats/1 year; Power BI Pro 10 seats/1 year; Visual Studio 5 seats (until time in the MfS portal ends); Power Apps & Power Automate 10 seats/1 year; Dynamics 365 Customer Service & Sales Enterprise 10 seats/1 year; Dynamics 365 Team Member 25 seats/1 year; LinkedIn Premium Business 1 seat at 75% off for 4 months; 'Startup Cluster' short-term GPU access (ND H100, NDm A100 VMs, subject to availability); higher Azure OpenAI quota limits; ACA serverless GPU quota (2–7 day approval); 24/7 Azure Standard Support (normally ~$1,200/year); dedicated MfS point of contact; go-to-market benefits (Microsoft Marketplace, co-sell).
