Apply without an investor code → $200 starter credits immediately, no application form (identity via personal LinkedIn). With a 10-character investor-network referral code → $1,000 starter credits immediately while the application is reviewed (~3 business days); approval unlocks expanded credits. Credits then auto-progress in tiers — no separate applications: $5,000 after business verification; $25,000 after 5+ distinct Azure workloads in active use for a sustained ~60 days (each workload must spend >$1/month for the whole 60 days; one-time two-year extension of credit validity at this tier); $50,000 at 7+ workloads; $150,000 at 10+ workloads plus ~$3,000/month Azure usage sustained ~60 days. Milestones can be skipped straight if requirements are met. Program support, AI capabilities (Microsoft Foundry models), and go-to-market resources are included.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage B2B software/AI/technology startups; funding stage pre-seed through Series B; at or working toward MVP; privately held independent companies (not subsidiaries); headquartered in a region where MfS operates (country choice at signup cannot be changed; if your country is absent, the offer is not available). Sign-in must use a personal consumer email (work/school/company domains rejected); phone number; registered business name matching legal documents; payment card is entered at Azure account creation (spend protection; converts to pay-as-you-go when credits are used up or expired).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups → 'Get started now'. 2) Sign in with a personal Microsoft account. 3) No code: 'Continue without a code' → 'Verify with LinkedIn' → 3-step Azure account setup (profile, billing address, payment card) → '$200 in Azure credits' unlocked. With code: enter the 10-char investor code → verify email + phone → business info form → submit → $1,000 credits immediately, approval within ~3 business days. Credits are managed in the MfS portal (portal.startups.microsoft.com); usage is auto-evaluated monthly.
  - *Expiry:* Per program FAQ guidance: credits must be activated within 90 days of accepting the program agreement; once activated they are valid up to 2 years with no extensions — except the one-time two-year extension awarded at the $25K milestone (per official docs).
  - *Gotchas:* Contrary to 'no card' expectations, the application flow requires a card at account creation (identity verification + billing above credit). 'Up to $150,000' is a ceiling, not a day-one grant — day one is $200 (or $1,000 with an investor code). Marketing pages still show stale 2025-track copy: /free/students advertises 'Azure for Startups: $1,000 instantly, up to $5,000, 90/180-day validity', which is the retired self-service track. The $150K figure in the Azure portal is a generic ceiling indicator, not a grant; additional credits are applied automatically (no 'redeem more' button exists). All activation must use the same personal Microsoft account registered at signup.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/application
- **Microsoft for Startups — Investor Network (enhanced track)** (medium) — Startups referred by Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partners (VCs, accelerators, incubators, universities; the partner list is not public) get the enhanced experience on top of the base program: dedicated Microsoft point of contact, deeper Microsoft Marketplace and co-sell support, and access to additional Startup credits beyond the standard program (up to the $150,000 ceiling). Under the current docs the investor path starts with $1,000 in credits immediately and unlocks up to $150,000 after application approval. (The interim 2025 track had started at $100,000 for investor-backed startups.)
  - *Eligibility:* Same base MfS eligibility (pre-seed–Series B, B2B, privately held, supported region) plus a 10-character referral code from a participating investor/accelerator/university.
  - *How to claim:* Ask your investor/accelerator for the 10-character referral code → apply at https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups with the code.
  - *Expiry:* Same activation/validity rules as base MfS (activate within 90 days; valid up to 2 years; 2-year extension at the $25K tier).
  - *Gotchas:* Extra credit amounts above the standard tiers are investor-dependent and not standardized publicly; the partner list is unpublished, so you must get the code from the investor directly.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/blog/microsoft-for-startups-introduces-a-clearer-path-from-ai-development-to-enterprise-growth/
- **Azure for Education — faculty dev-tool licenses (no cloud credits)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — Education-licensing front: Azure for Education gives teachers/educators free access to Microsoft and Azure development tools and software licenses for teaching (Visual Studio Community, SQL Server Developer/Standard editions, Windows Server, Hyper-V, R Client/Server, VS Code, etc.) — included even inside Azure for Students subscriptions. No cloud credits are part of the education program; faculty who want credits must take the standard $200 free account (card required) or request an academic grant/sponsorship (custom spending-limit Azure Sponsorship subscriptions, e.g. for competitions, via Azure for Education support).
  - *Eligibility:* Educators in K-12 and higher education; verification through school/organization. Not a credit offer.
  - *How to claim:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/for-education/ for the dev-tools program; sponsorship/academic grants via Azure for Education support (azureforeducation.microsoft.com contact).
  - *Expiry:* Licenses valid while the account is a qualifying educator/student.
  - *Gotchas:* No credits — if the research question is 'free compute for faculty', the answer is the standard $200 trial card or a bespoke sponsorship request, not an education program. This page was not directly re-fetched today, hence low confidence.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/for-education/
- **Bare-metal & dedicated servers front — no dedicated free offer** (high) — Azure publishes no free tier, free trial allotment, or always-free amount for dedicated hosts or bare-metal VM SKUs. The VPS front is covered by the free 750 h/month burstable VMs + $200 trial credit; the managed-database front by the free Flexible Server/Managed Instance/always-free Cosmos and SQL serverless amounts. Dedicated/bare-metal capacity is reachable only by spending trial credits or Microsoft-for-Startups credits on eligible services — i.e., the 'free' path for a dedicated server is a credit, not a free allotment.
  - *Eligibility:* Via the $200 free-account trial (card required) or MfS startup credits (card at account creation).
  - *How to claim:* No standalone claim; use a trial or MfS credit in a paid subscription.
  - *Expiry:* As the underlying credit (30-day trial credit; MfS activation + up to 2-year validity).
  - *Gotchas:* Do not plan runways around free dedicated capacity — it does not exist; credit ceilings are hard caps, and beyond them it is straight PAYG.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **Azure SQL Database (serverless) — 'Free forever' tier** (high) — 100,000 vCore-seconds/month of Azure SQL Database serverless compute plus 32 GB storage, at $0 while within the free amounts, with no time limit (monthly recurring allowance). 100,000 vCore-sec ≈ 3.33 vCore-hours/day: a continuously running 1-vCore database ≈ 27.8 hrs/month; a 4-vCore database ≈ 6.9 hrs/month. Verified 2026-10-22 on the official /free/ page and the student-offer page; the dedicated SQL serverless pricing-details URL now 404s (Azure has reorganized pricing URLs).
  - *Eligibility:* All new and existing Azure customers (free account, student account, or pay-as-you-go); no credit card needed to stay inside the free amounts (signing up for an Azure account may still require a payment method unless using the no-CC student route). Not available in sovereign-cloud regions (US Government, Azure China, Azure Germany).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an Azure account (free, student, or PAYG). 2) Deploy an Azure SQL database with serverless compute. 3) Stay within 100,000 vCore-seconds/month and 32 GB storage; compute auto-stops idle. No separate enrollment needed.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — the free allowance recurs monthly for the life of the account (student accounts renew yearly while enrolled).
  - *Gotchas:* Compute beyond 100,000 vCore-sec/month and storage beyond 32 GB bill at PAYG rates; backups beyond included storage bill; data persists while compute auto-stops, but the DB is not 'free' if you exceed allowances. Not the old provisioned F0 'free' SKU — that SKU was retired in favor of this serverless allowance.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **Azure Cosmos DB — 'Free forever' tier** (high) — 1,000 RU/s provisioned throughput plus 25 GB storage per month at $0 while within free amounts, no time limit. Official pricing page (read 2026-10-22): 'New accounts are eligible to receive 1,000 request units per second (RU/s) throughput and 25 GBs storage per month with Azure Cosmos DB free tier.' Applies to the RU-based APIs: NoSQL, MongoDB (RU), Cassandra, Gremlin, and Table. Autoscale minimum is 1,000 RU/s (scales 100–1,000 hourly).
  - *Eligibility:* All Azure customers (listed on the official page as a free-forever service; pricing page phrases it as 'new accounts'); no credit card needed to stay inside the free amounts; sovereign-cloud regions excluded.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an Azure account. 2) Create a Cosmos DB account on an RU-based API (NoSQL/MongoDB/Cassandra/Gremlin/Table). 3) Provision 1,000 RU/s and keep storage ≤ 25 GB.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — recurring monthly allowance for the life of the account.
  - *Gotchas:* Throughput above 1,000 RU/s, storage above 25 GB, data egress, and overage requests bill at PAYG rates; no separate 12-month Cosmos serverless allowance (400,000 RU + 32 GB) appears on the current pricing page — the live free tier is the provisioned 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB one. Staying on standard provisioned 1,000 RU/s is the safest configuration to remain inside free amounts.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/cosmos-db/
- **Azure free account — $200 credit (30 days) + 12-month free services** (high) — $200 credit usable on Azure services within 30 days of account creation, plus 12 months of free allowances on 20+ services (first-usage amounts). 12-month database/data allowances include: Azure Database for PostgreSQL Flexible Server 750 hrs B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; MySQL Flexible Server 750 hrs B1MS + 32 GB + 32 GB backup; Azure SQL Managed Instance 720 vCore-hours of a 4- or 8-vCore General Purpose instance incl. 64 GB data + 64 GB backup; Azure Container Registry 1 Standard registry with 100 GB. If you convert to PAYG early, remaining credit stays available for the full 30 days from creation.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only (one free-account offer per new customer). Phone number plus a non-prepaid credit/debit card required at sign-up; only credit cards accepted in Hong Kong and Brazil. Sign-in with Microsoft or GitHub account. Sovereign-cloud products excluded (US Government, China, Germany).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.com/free with a Microsoft or GitHub account. 2) Provide phone + non-prepaid card (a temporary ~$1 authorization may be placed and reversed). 3) Choose the 'free account' (not pay-as-you-go) option at sign-up so the $200 + 12-month allowances attach.
  - *Expiry:* Credit expires 30 days from account creation; 12-month allowances end 12 months from creation. Account then automatically converts to pay-as-you-go (card on file), or services are disabled if you don't add payment.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a trial, not a free tier: after 30 days or when $200 is spent, all usage bills at PAYG. Credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers, Azure Spot VMs, or combined with Hybrid Benefit. Data/egress beyond free amounts bill even during the 12-month window. 12-month VM allowance: 750 hours each of B1s, B2pts v2 (ARM) and B2ats v2 (AMD) burstable VMs.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **Azure managed databases 12-month free quotas (PostgreSQL/MySQL Flexible Server, SQL Managed Instance)** (high) — The 12-month free quotas, available on both the new-customer free account and the student offer: Azure Database for PostgreSQL Flexible Server — 750 hours/month of Burstable B1MS instance + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup storage; Azure Database for MySQL Flexible Server — identical (750 hrs B1MS + 32 GB + 32 GB backup); Azure SQL Managed Instance — 720 vCore-hours/month of a 4-vCore or 8-vCore General Purpose instance, incl. 64 GB data storage and 64 GB backup storage. 750 hours ≈ one continuously running small server. (Azure SQL serverless 'free forever' is a separate, perpetual allowance — see its own finding.)
  - *Eligibility:* New-customer free accounts and Azure for Students accounts; one account per person for students; sovereign-cloud regions excluded; PostgreSQL/MySQL Flexible Server B1MS is only offered in select regions (region availability varies).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create an eligible free or student account. 2) Deploy PostgreSQL or MySQL Flexible Server on B1MS (or SQL Managed Instance on 4/8-vCore General Purpose). 3) Monitor meters in the Azure portal so usage stays within 750 hrs / 720 vCore-hours and the included storage each month.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation; student accounts renew annually while still a full-time student; then PAYG pricing applies.
  - *Gotchas:* Monthly quotas do not roll over; any hour/vCore-hour or GB beyond the allowance bills at PAYG rates immediately; backups beyond included 32/64 GB bill; there is no 12-month free allowance for managed instances of MySQL/PostgreSQL (only Flexible Server / the listed SKUs).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit + 12-month free services + always-free (no credit card)** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on the official student-offer page: $100 credit usable on most Azure products within 12 months (not $200 — the $200 figure is the general new-customer trial) + free monthly amounts on 20+ popular services for 12 months (including the PostgreSQL/MySQL B1MS and SQL Managed Instance quotas above) + always-free services (SQL serverless 100,000 vCore-sec/month + 32 GB; Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB; Azure DevOps 5 users; Functions 1M requests; App Service; AKS management; AI Search 50 MB; and more) + free developer tools via the Azure Education Hub (Visual Studio Community, SQL Server, Windows Server). No credit card required; one account per person.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students only. A school email is the gate: 'Use your school email to sign up and renew yearly while you're a student.' The page documents no alternate Microsoft Learn enrollment route and no credit card required. New Azure customers; accounts are 'new Azure customers only' for the 12-month monthly amounts.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.com/free/students using your school email (no payment method). 2) Receive the $100 credit (12-month validity) and the 12-month free monthly amounts. 3) Renew the subscription annually with your school email while enrolled; free-forever items continue without renewal.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 12 months. 12-month service allowances: 12 months, then annual renewal required while a full-time student (renew by email prompt; otherwise subscription and products are disabled). Always-free services: no expiry. One account per person.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers; after the 12-month window or on renewal lapse, moving to PAYG requires adding a payment method or the subscription is disabled; students who graduate or leave full-time status lose renewal eligibility. Note the $100 student credit is distinct from the $200 new-customer trial credit — an existing free-account holder is not a 'new' customer.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **GitHub Student Developer Pack — Azure benefits (secondary claim route)** (high) — Verified 2026-10-22 on the official GitHub Education page: for verified students 18+, the pack includes 'Free access to 25+ Microsoft Azure cloud services plus $100 in Azure credit' (no credit card). For students aged 13–17, a separate 'Microsoft Azure (for ages 13-17)' benefit gives named free services instead of a monetary credit: Azure App Service, Azure Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL database (in-app), Application Insights, and Azure DevOps. Claiming the benefit provisions the equivalent of an Azure student account.
  - *Eligibility:* A free GitHub account plus GitHub student verification (18+ tier for the $100 credit; 13–17 tier gets restricted free services, no credit). No credit card required per the GitHub page. GitHub's verification can accept school-issued credentials, so it is a practical route for students whose school lacks a verifiable .edu address.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up for the GitHub Student Developer Pack at education.github.com/pack. 2) Complete GitHub's student verification. 3) Redeem the Azure benefit, which creates the Azure student account with the $100 credit and 12-month free services.
  - *Expiry:* GitHub's page prints no expiry window; the $100 Azure credit mirrors the Azure for Students offer (usable within 12 months).
  - *Gotchas:* The 13–17 tier has no monetary credit; the $100/25+ services benefit is for 18+. Exact credit expiry and 12-month allowance terms live in the resulting Azure account, not on GitHub's page.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Microsoft for Startups (Founders Hub) — up to $150,000 Azure credits** (medium) — The official Microsoft for Startups page (read 2026-10-22) states startups get 'up to $150,000 in credits' that 'can be applied across eligible Azure services to support your startup from early development through production workloads,' plus M365 licenses, GitHub Enterprise access, and co-sell support via the Microsoft network. The program now runs through the 'Founders Hub' portal with tiered Azure credits: an entry tier requiring no VC/accelerator referral (historically ~$1,500 Azure credits), intermediate tiers (community reports of ~$25,000), and a 'Scale' level unlocking $150,000, typically investor-referred (investor network: Y Combinator, Techstars, 500 Global, MassChallenge, Plug and Play, Seedcamp, Antler, Founder Institute, and others).
  - *Eligibility:* Private, software-based startups in program-available markets. Community/third-party reports (not republished on the pages fetched today) put the classic thresholds at less than 5 years since founding and less than ~$30M raised; entry tiers are open without a VC referral, higher tiers require referral from the investor network. Tiers advance via an automated 'engagement score' (e.g., consuming ~50% of the current tier's credits and using a mix of services).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at microsoft.com/en-us/startups via 'Get started now' into the Founders Hub portal (sign in with Microsoft account). 2) Complete the application: company name/website, industry, stage (pre-seed/seed/Series A+), product description. 3) Review takes days to weeks; grants appear in the Founders Hub portal, where 'Level Up' requests to the $150k tier are also submitted.
  - *Expiry:* Credits are granted per tier with tier-specific expiry windows (typically 6–12 months per tier; not stated on the official page fetched today). Extensions are generally not granted; the stated path is leveling up to the next tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Headline 'up to $150,000' is from the official page today; the specific tier dollar amounts ($1,500 entry, ~$25,000 mid, $150,000 scale) and the age/funding caps come from third-party/community sources, so treat them as medium/low confidence. Credits do not apply to Azure Marketplace offers; the program is a customer-acquisition channel, so post-credit consumption is what Microsoft monetizes; co-sell/transaction fees (3% new / 1.5% renewal) attach to the co-sell program, not to credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups
- **Azure free account: $200 credit, 30 days** (high) — New Azure customers get $200 credit usable for 30 days (or until credit is exhausted), plus the 12-month free service amounts and 65+ always-free services on top. After 30 days / credit exhaustion the account and services are DISABLED (not auto-converted); you must move to pay-as-you-go to continue. Unused credit stays available for the full 30 days from creation. Credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers or Azure Spot VMs. Sovereign cloud products in US Government, Azure China, and Azure Germany are excluded. No $100 12-month credit is listed on the current pages — the standard trial is $200/30 days only.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only (one free account per new customer). Requires phone number, a credit or debit card (non-prepaid), and a Microsoft or GitHub account. A temporary ~$1 authorization is placed on the card and reversed on verification; the card is not otherwise charged. Only credit cards accepted in Hong Kong and Brazil. Offer unavailable in countries not on Microsoft's list; 'If your country is not listed, the offer is not available in your region.'
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free → verify phone + card (temporary $1 hold) → $200 credit active for 30 days → within 30 days move to pay-as-you-go (portal: Subscriptions → Upgrade subscription) to keep the 12-month free amounts; otherwise services are disabled at day 30.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 30 days from account creation (unused credit lost). Free service amounts: 12 months after sign-up for the trial account. Renewal: none — after 12 months, used services bill at PAYG rates.
  - *Gotchas:* VPS angle: the 12-month free VM allowance covers B1s, B2pts v2 (ARM) and B2ats v2 (AMD) burstable VMs only — 750 hours each per month. That is roughly 24h/day for one VM; larger/Standard VMs are NOT covered. Data transfer/egress beyond free allowances and storage beyond free quotas bill at PAYG rates even in the trial. Account is disabled (not billed) if you do nothing — but resources are inaccessible until upgrade.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **12-month free service amounts (new customers)** (high) — Free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months: VMs 750 hours/month each of B1s, B2pts v2 (ARM-based), B2ats v2 (AMD-based) burstable VMs; Azure Container Registry: 1 Standard registry, 100 GB storage, 10 webhooks; PostgreSQL Flexible Server: 750 hours Burstable B1MS + 32 GB storage + 32 GB backup; MySQL Flexible Server: same 750 hours B1MS + 32 GB + 32 GB; Azure SQL Managed Instance: 720 vCore hours of 4 or 8 vCore General Purpose (64 GB data + 64 GB backup); Azure Vision (Foundry Tools): 5,000 transactions per S1/S2/S3 tier. Can be consumed in any region where the service is available and split across instances (e.g. 5 VMs × 150 h). Services in use after 12 months continue running and bill at PAYG.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only — 'not previously had an Azure account or received 12 months of free services.' NOT currently available to customers who sign up directly for pay-as-you-go in China and India. Trial signups must move to pay-as-you-go within 30 days to keep receiving them.
  - *How to claim:* No separate claim — included automatically when creating any Azure account (free trial or PAYG, new customer). Create resources via the 'Free services' page in the Azure portal with free tiers selected to avoid accidental billing.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months from account creation; unused monthly amounts do not roll over; after 12 months everything in use bills at PAYG rates. No renewal.
  - *Gotchas:* For a free VPS: 750 h/month is the total shared across B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 — a single always-on B1s VM works for ~12 months, but nothing above B-series burstable is free. 'Always-free' services (65+) do NOT include VMs, so a pure PAYG account beyond 12 months has no free compute; only the free service tiers listed remain. Egress/storage beyond free quotas bill.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/free-services
- **Always-free services (65+, all accounts)** (high) — Permanent free monthly amounts for every Azure account: App Service (10 web/API apps, 1 GB storage, 1 hour/day compute), Functions (1 million requests), Container Apps (180,000 vCPU-s, 360,000 GiB-s, 2M requests), Static Web Apps (0.5 GB/app, 100 GB bandwidth/subscription), AKS cluster management (nodes bill), Cosmos DB (1,000 RU/s + 25 GB), Azure SQL serverless (100,000 vCore-s + 32 GB/month), DocumentDB (32 GB cluster), Azure Data Factory (5 low-frequency activities), API Management Consumption (1M calls/month), Logic Apps Consumption (4,000 actions), Notification Hubs (1M pushes), IoT Hub (8,000 msgs/day), AI/Foundry services (Speech 0.5M chars, Translator 2M chars, Document Intelligence 500 pages S0, AI Search 50 MB/10k docs/3 indexes, Vision/Content Safety tiers), Azure DevOps (5 users), Azure Machine Learning (free, egress may apply), plus free/unlimited management services (Monitor control plane, Arc, Advisor, Migrate, Cost Management, Lighthouse, etc.).
  - *Eligibility:* All Azure customers, no verification beyond having an account; no credit card needed if usage stays within free amounts.
  - *How to claim:* Automatic with any Azure account; create resources on the free tiers. This is the realistic 'always-free tier' for lightweight workloads (serverless/PaaS) — but NOT for VPS: no free VM tier exists in the always-free set.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry while the account exists; monthly allowances reset each month. Cloud Shell's 5 GB free storage in Azure Files lasts 12 months only.
  - *Gotchas:* App Service '1 hour per day' makes it usable as a very small always-on worker but it's not a VM. Egress, premium storage and anything above free tier limits bill. AKS is 'free' only for cluster management — node VMs bill.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/
- **Azure for Students: $100 credit, 12 months, renewable yearly** (high) — $100 in Azure credit usable within 12 months, plus 20+ 12-month free service amounts and 65+ always-free services; includes Azure OpenAI/Foundry access and free dev-tool downloads via the Azure Education Hub (Visual Studio, SQL Server, Windows Server, etc.). Unused credit cannot carry over between months/subscriptions. If the $100 runs out early the subscription is disabled; you get a 90-day window after the free period to upgrade to PAYG, otherwise products are decommissioned. One subscription per person, non-transferable, cannot combine with other offers. Not available for MOOCs or for-profit training. Credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers.

Track remaining credit in the Azure Sponsorships portal.

A Microsoft Q&A thread confirms $100 exhaustion disables the subscription (in markets where the credit converts to local-currency spend, e.g. ~₹8,500 in India).   - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Azure for Students Starter (no-credit-card, no-$100 tier)** (medium) — A second education offer: 'Azure for Students Starter' — academic email verification, no credit card, access to selected free Azure services (e.g. App Service free tiers) without the $100 credit.   - *Expiry:* Free service amounts run while the subscription is active (12 months for the new-customer amounts). No stated credit because there is no credit pool.
  - *Gotchas:* Exact per-service Starter allowances are not itemized on the current /free/students page (Microsoft's academic content describes it as 'use select Azure products like App Services for free') — treat specific limits as unconfirmed. Not a substitute for the $100 credit; best used as a path to the standard free account ($200/30 days).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Azure for Startups (self-service credit offer, up to $5,000)** (high) — The self-service startup credit offer (open track of the two-track system introduced July 1, 2025): $1,000 in Azure credits instantly, no application required, valid 90 days; up to $4,000 more (total $5,000) unlocked after business verification, valid 180 days. Full Azure catalog access up to credit limits; after expiry/usage the account moves to pay-as-you-go. No dedicated support — self-serve.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups that are NEW to Azure (new Azure customers only). Business verification = confirmation the startup is a registered legal entity. A credit card is required only for identity verification and for usage beyond the credits.
  - *How to claim:* No application: create an Azure account under the Azure for Startups offer (linked from azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students and microsoft.com/startups) → $1,000 credit active for 90 days → complete business verification → additional up to $4,000 for 180 days.
  - *Expiry:* Initial $1,000: 90 days. Post-verification $4,000: 180 days. Then PAYG. No renewal; cannot combine with other offers unless Microsoft permits.
  - *Gotchas:* This is the post-July-2025 self-service track — it replaced the old unfunded-startup tiers (former L1-L4 up to $150k). Note the marketing card says $1,000 instant, while the Microsoft for Startups program application (separate, see other finding) starts at $200 — they are different entry points.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Microsoft for Startups program: milestone credits up to $150,000 (2026 structure)** (high) — Current (2026) milestone structure from the official getting-started doc (updated 2026-08-05): apply at startups.microsoft.com → M1 $200 (sign up + personal-LINKEDIN identity verification, no business accounts), M2 $5,000 (business verification — registered legal entity), M3 $25,000 (5+ Azure workloads active ~60 days), M4 $50,000 (7+ workloads ~60 days), M5 $150,000 (10+ workloads + ~$3,000/month Azure usage sustained ~60 days). Milestones can be skipped; at $25K you get a one-time two-year credit-use extension. Workload = a distinct actively-used Azure service (VMs, AI models, databases, hosting). Investor-network track (introduced July 2025): startups referred by an affiliated investor/accelerator/university start at $100,000 with dedicated support. Pegasus (invitation-only CSM support) continues unchanged.
  - *Eligibility:* Startups applying through the program; no publicly stated startup-age or funding cap in the current 2026 docs (the older program required companies founded <2 years — that explicit criterion is not on the current page). Personal LinkedIn at M1; registered legal entity for M2.

  - *Expiry:* 2-year use window at the $25K milestone (one-time extension). Higher-tier availability requires sustained ~$3,000/month usage for 60 days — credits subsidize, not replace, that spend. No stated hard expiry for the top tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Every workload must stay above $1 spend for the full 60-day window or the count resets. 'Up to $150k' effectively requires ~$3k/month real spend across 10+ services — it is not free infrastructure for a small VPS. GPU availability has been reported as chronically constrained by participants. Program rules have changed 3 times since 2025 (see sunset notes) — re-verify before planning.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs
- **Azure for Nonprofits: $2,000 grant annually** (medium) — Registered nonprofits receive a $2,000 Azure grant annually, plus broader Dynamics 365 / Microsoft 365 grants and discounts.   - *Expiry:* Annual grant; renews yearly. Not a trial — it is a recurring sponsorship.
  - *Gotchas:* Not relevant for personal VPS use; listed because it is a distinct standing free/low-cost offer on the Azure side. Grant mechanics (channel, exact process) are confirmed at headline level only.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/offers

It is a program for existing/migrating customers rather than a sign-up free offer for new accounts; the offers page does not publish a fixed credit amount today.

  - *How to claim:* Contact Microsoft / apply via the Azure Accelerate page; credits allocated case-by-case.
  - *Expiry:* Not stated on the offers page.
  - *Gotchas:* Treat as supplementary/uncertain — no concrete amount verified today; useful only for migration scenarios, not for a free VPS.

- **Azure DevOps Basic plan (free tier, perpetual)** (high) — All facts read from https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/devops/ today. 'Stakeholders (Free)' tier: free read-only participants. 'Basic' plan: first 5 users free, then $6/user/month; 1 Microsoft-hosted CI/CD parallel job with 1,800 build minutes/month; 1 self-hosted parallel job with unlimited minutes; extra Microsoft-hosted parallel job $40/month; extra self-hosted parallel job $15/month (unlimited minutes); Azure Artifacts 2 GiB free per org, then $2/GiB (2-10), $1/GiB (10-100), $0.50/GiB (100-1k), $0.25/GiB (1k+); unlimited private Git repos; Azure Boards work-item tracking and Kanban included. 'Basic + Test plans' is $52/user/month with a 30-day free trial (adds test planning/tracking/execution, browser tests, UAT, centralized reporting). Visual Studio subscribers get free Azure DevOps access and don't count against the 5 free users; Azure DevOps Basic is included in GitHub Enterprise; 'Basic' also carries a 'free offer' from Individual Services.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone; no credit card required for the Basic plan; per-org free allowance. Region: no restriction stated on the pricing page.
  - *How to claim:* Create an Azure/Microsoft account, go to dev.azure.com, create an organization: first 5 users + 1 MS-hosted job (1,800 min/mo) + 2GB artifacts activate automatically; buy extra users/jobs as needed. For Test plans, start the 30-day trial then $52/user/mo.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; the Basic free allowance is perpetual and build minutes reset monthly. Paid Test-plan trial expires after 30 days.
  - *Gotchas:* 1,800 MS-hosted minutes do not roll over monthly; the free 2GB artifact cap is per organization; extra MS-hosted parallel jobs are $40/month (big jump); Basic + Test plans is $52/user/month after the 30-day trial; as of 2025-2026 the page adds GitHub-hosted Mac agents (per-minute, no parallel job) and 'GitHub AI Credits for Azure DevOps' (Copilot autofix, code review) billed per credit; 99.9% SLA applies to paid users only.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/devops/
- **Azure Free Account trial (new-customer credits + 12-month free services)** (high) — Read from https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/ today: $200 credit usable within 30 days for new customers, PLUS free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (e.g., 750 hours each of B1s, B2pts v2 (Arm) and B2ats v2 (AMD) VMs; 750 hours PostgreSQL/MySQL Flexible Server B1MS with 32 GB storage; Standard Container Registry 100 GB + 10 webhooks; 720 vCore-hours SQL Managed Instance), PLUS 65+ always-free services (Azure Functions 1M requests/mo; Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s provisioned + 25 GB; AKS cluster management free; Azure SQL 100,000 vCore-seconds + 32 GB serverless). This credit/always-free coverage spans Azure consumption services including Azure Pipelines/DevOps compute beyond the free 1,800-min Basic allowance.

  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (contrast with the student offer); account auto-converts to pay-as-you-go after credit expiry — any unused credit is lost; 65+ always-free services have per-service monthly caps (excess is billed); sovereign-cloud regions excluded; this is a trial-credit offer, not a perpetual free tier.

- **Microsoft for Startups — up to $150,000 startup credits (single-entry, milestone-based, as of 2026)** (high) — Read from current official Learn docs (updated 2026-08-05 and 2026-10-09): single entry point at startups.microsoft.com. On signup you get $200 in 'Startup credits' immediately (investor-referral path: $1,000 immediately); then automatic milestone unlocks up to $150,000: Milestone 1 $200 = Azure signup + personal-Linkekin identity verify; M2 $5,000 = business verification (registered legal entity); M3 $25,000 = 5+ distinct Azure workloads in active use over ~60 days; M4 $50,000 = 7+ workloads ~60 days; M5 $150,000 = 10+ workloads plus ~$3,000/month Azure usage sustained ~60 days. The 60-day clock requires every counted workload to stay above $1 spend for all 60 days; milestones can be skipped (qualify directly for higher tiers). At the $25K milestone you get a one-time two-year extension to use your credits. Investor Network (VC/accelerator/university) 10-character referral codes unlock higher-tier benefits earlier and expanded/third-party perks. Credits cover eligible Azure consumption (incl. AKS and eligible Microsoft Foundry model usage); GitHub usage not covered out of the box. Other benefits: Azure AI/Foundry access, Microsoft Marketplace co-sell, dedicated program support; review within ~3 business days.

Applicant needs a personal consumer Microsoft account with no prior Azure account, personal (non-school/work-domain) email, phone, LinkedIn (personal account, identity verify), and payment/credit card info at Azure account creation (credit card IS required for the sponsored subscription).
  - *How to claim:* Go to startups.microsoft.com (or the www.microsoft.com/en-us/startups landing 'Get started now'); sign in with a personal MS account; optionally enter your investor's 10-character referral code; complete the 3-step Azure account creation (profile/address/payment) to unlock $200 (or $1,000 on the investor path); application review ~3 business days; credits activate in the portal.
  - *Expiry:* Credits have program-defined durations (one-time 2-year extension at the $25K milestone); the sponsored subscription auto-converts to pay-as-you-go when credits are exhausted or expire. Existing activated credits from the old 2025 programs remained valid until they expired after the 2025-07-01 change.
  - *Gotchas:* Workload-count trap: any of the 5/7/10 workloads dropping below $1/month spend during the 60-day window resets that milestone; $150K requires ~$3K/month real spend anyway; lifetime $350K free-credit cap blocks repeat users; credits don't cover Marketplace, support plans, or GitHub (needs Azure linked as GitHub payment method); country/region chosen at signup can't be changed later and some countries are ineligible; personal email only — school/company domains rejected; LinkedIn personal account required (no business accounts).
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview(milestones:.../getting-started-mfs;applicationsteps:.../application)
- **Azure Free Account — $200 credit for 30 days + 12-month free services (B1S / B2pts v2 / B2ats v2, 750 hrs/month)** (high) — Confirmed today on official pages: $200 credit (in your billing currency) usable for 30 days on any Azure service except third-party Marketplace purchases. From signup up to 12 months: free monthly amounts of ~25+ services, including 750 hours/month EACH of the three free burstable VM SKUs — B1S, B2pts v2 (ARM-based), B2ats v2 (AMD-based), Linux or Windows. 750 hours can be split across instances (e.g. 5 VMs x 150h) and created in any region where B-series is available. After day 30 only the free-limited services remain free; unused $200 credit is lost at day 30 unless you upgrade to pay-as-you-go before 30 days (then credit stays usable for the full 30-day window). Must move to pay-as-you-go within 30 days or account/services are disabled. 12-month offer is new-customers-only (never had an Azure account / 12-month benefits before) and NOT available to customers who sign up directly for pay-as-you-go in China and India.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers. Credit card or debit card (non-prepaid) + phone number REQUIRED at signup ($1 temporary auth hold, reversed; credit cards only in HK and Brazil). No .edu needed. Not available to existing PAYG signups in China/India for the 12-month benefit.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.microsoft.com for 'Azure Free Account' with phone + card. 2) In portal, use the 'Free services' page (go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=859151) so free tiers are preselected. 3) Create resources within 750h/month VM limits; convert to pay-as-you-go before day 30 to keep the 12-month free tier.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 30 days from account creation (lost if unused unless early PAYG upgrade). Free services: 12 months from account creation, then services decommission/subscription disabled; no renewal of the trial itself.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required (unlike student offers). Free VM hours exclude egress over free bandwidth, public IP and disk charges — classic surprise-bill source. Don't create resources outside the 'Free services' page: free tiers are not default-selected elsewhere, you get billed. After day 30 without PAYG upgrade the whole account is disabled; resuming requires PAYG. HK/Brazil prepaid cards rejected.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/create-free-services(alsohttps://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account)
- **65+ always-free Azure services (incl. notebook/ML-adjacent: Synapse Spark, Databricks, AI/ML categories)** (medium) — The free-services overview page (read today) states '65+ always-free services with an Azure account' with 'free monthly amounts... for as long as you have an Azure account', spanning AI/ML, compute, databases, IoT, management. The per-service limit tables on that page are JS-rendered and could not be extracted today, so the specific figures below are the long-standing published limits, unconfirmed in today's fetch: Azure Synapse Spark SQL pool DW1000c 250 hrs/month, Azure Databricks 32 SQL DBU/month, Data Factory 5,000 slicing units/month, App Service 1 shared instance + 60k req/day, Functions 1M req + 400k GB-s, Storage 5 GB LRS + 5 GB premium, Cosmos DB 500 RU/s + 400 GB, 100 GB egress/month, App Insights 5 MB ingest/day. Always-free is available with ANY account, including pay-as-you-go accounts — you do not need the trial.

  - *How to claim:* Sign up for any Azure account; create services via the 'Free services' page in portal selecting the free tier. No separate application.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite — free monthly amounts continue for life of account; amounts reset monthly; exceeding limits triggers PAYG billing.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free confusion: the 12-month tier is a separate, larger allotment that expires at 12 months; the always-free tier persists. Free Spark/Databricks limits are small — fine for notebook experiments, not sustained GPU/ML training. Outbound data and data at rest beyond free storage bill separately. Per-service figures above were not re-verifiable today (JS-rendered table), treat exact numbers as medium-confidence until checked at azure.microsoft.com/pricing/free-services in a browser.

- **Free GPU / ML-training & notebooks compute: what actually exists** (medium) — Azure has NO free-tier GPU VMs and no free Azure Machine Learning compute in the free account. The only free paths for GPU/ML work: (1) the $200 trial credit (30 days) can buy any compute incl. GPU VMs (e.g. NC/NV/A-series) — a few hours of a mid GPU VM is typical of what $200 buys, exact durations vary by SKU/region; (2) always-free notebooks/data engines: Synapse Spark SQL DW1000c 250 hrs/month and Databricks 32 SQL DBU/month (medium confidence, see above) — CPU-based, not GPU; (3) 750 hrs/month B-series burstable VMs for light CPU training/inference. For sustained GPU/ML training the realistic free routes are the startup credits (up to $150K, finding 8) or Azure Spot/standard pricing — there is no recurring free GPU offering at Azure as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Eligibility:* Same as each underlying offer: new customers for the $200 credit; any account for always-free Spark/Databricks.
  - *How to claim:* Use the $200 credit within 30 days on GPU VMs; enable always-free Spark/Databricks via the 'Free services' page in the portal.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 30 days. Spark/Databricks free amounts: monthly, indefinite.
  - *Gotchas:* GPU hours under the credit are finite and CPU-free tiers can't replace them; egress and managed-disk I/O billed; Databricks free DBUs cover SQL warehouse only, not full DBU workspace runs; don't confuse trial (30d) with always-free.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/create-free-services
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit for 12 months, no credit card required** (high) — Confirmed today: $100 in Azure credits usable within the first 12 months (non-rollover), plus 'select free services' (FAQ states the offer includes 25+ free services incl. compute/network/storage/databases). - **Microsoft for Startups — base $1,000 (90 days) + $4,000 (180 days) Azure credits; up to $150,000 via investor referrals** (high) — Confirmed today on Learn (page dated 2026-05-21/22) and the marketing landing page: single-entry program at startups.microsoft.com. (b) Investor offer (referral code from a partner in the Microsoft for Startups Investor Network — investor, accelerator, incubator, angel network, or similar): most startups begin with $100,000 in Azure credits with the opportunity to earn more; program 'unlocks up to $150K in Startup credits over time' as verified progress/adoption/sustained usage; landing page advertises 'up to $150,000 in credits'. CC is required for credit activation (identity verification + transition to PAYG).

- **Microsoft for Startups — Startup credits, milestone-based (the current Founders Hub program)** (high) — Read today (ms.date 2026-05-27): a single program now replaces the old Founders Hub tier system. You enter at Milestone 1 = up to $200 in Startup credits immediately after signing up for Azure + identity verification via a PERSONAL LinkedIn account. Milestone 2 = $5,000 after business verification (registered legal entity). Milestone 3 = $25,000 (5+ distinct Azure workloads each >= $1 spend, held ~60 continuous days; one-time 2-year credit-usage extension granted at this milestone). Milestone 4 = $50,000 (7 workloads, ~60 days). Milestone 5 = $150,000 (10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month Azure spend sustained ~60 days). Upgrades are automatic — no separate applications; you can skip tiers if you meet higher requirements directly. The marketing page still advertises 'up to $150,000 in credits'.
  - *Eligibility:* Privately held, for-profit; software-based product owned by the company; HQ in a country where Azure is available; no more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits; NOT Series C funding or later (tightened from Series D in 2025); not an educational institution, government org, consultancy or agency; no crypto mining. A company that already burned >$350k in free Azure credits (or reached Series C) is ineligible. No .edu requirement; 'open to anyone with an idea' per the signup page.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to startups.microsoft.com (Founders Hub) > Get started. 2) Submit application (personal LinkedIn at identity step). 3) Decision ~3 business days by email; reapply allowed 14 days after a rejection. 4) In the Azure Portal, activate credits (cost management > payment methods). 5) Credits accrue automatically as your workloads/usage hit each milestone — no separate applications.
  - *Expiry:* Activated credits are valid for up to 2 years from activation (per FAQ); no extensions except the one-time 2-year extension at the $25K milestone. You must activate within 90 days of accepting the program agreement or the offer lapses.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card IS required to activate credits (identity verification + pay-as-you-go fallback). Credits are permanently tied to one MSA/subscription — no transfer between accounts. Pay-as-you-go kicks in automatically when credits run out. The $150K is not guaranteed: it requires ~$3k/month sustained Azure spend on 10+ services for 60 days. Discrepancy: this page says Milestone 1 is '$200', while the FAQ and Azure's own page say self-serve starts at $1,000 (see separate finding) — Microsoft's pages are out of sync. Existing participants from the 2025 two-track system keep old credits until expiry, then convert to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs
- **Azure for Startups self-serve credits ($1,000 instant, up to $5,000) — no application** (high) — Confirmed on two pages read today: 'Up to $5,000 in Azure credits: $1,000 available immediately, plus up to $4,000 more after verification'. Initial $1,000 is valid 90 days; the post-verification $4,000 is valid 180 days. No application required — just signup. This matches the pre-known 'self-serve Azure for Startups' offer. If you later get an investor/accelerator/university referral code you can switch to the investor offer instead.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers only. .edu not required. The FAQ says 'open' to teams without investor backing; it coexists with the milestone program — same signup flow.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at startups.microsoft.com or Azure's 'Azure for Startups' offer (link from azure.microsoft.com/free/students or the MFS portal). 2) $1,000 credits apply instantly to your new Azure subscription. 3) Complete business verification to unlock the additional $4,000. No application, no review queue for the self-serve tier.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 valid 90 days from activation; $4,000 valid 180 days from business verification. After expiry: automatic conversion to pay-as-you-go.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card required for identity verification and for spend beyond credits ('spending protection' only covers the card from unauthorized billing). Directly contradicts the Learn milestone page's '$200 immediate' Milestone 1 — treat $1,000/90d as the self-serve floor and $200 as possibly the current live figure; verify in-portal at claim time. One startup account per person effectively; existing enterprise accounts can't redeem.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **Microsoft for Startups Investor Network track (~$100,000+ Azure credits, referral-gated)** (high) — From the MFS FAQ read today: investor-backed startups enter with 'most startups begin with $100,000 [in Azure credits], with the opportunity to earn more' plus Azure Standard Support, technical experts, go-to-market support, and free/discounted third-party benefits: GitHub Enterprise, LinkedIn Premium Business, Microsoft 365 Business Premium, Visual Studio Enterprise, Dynamics 365, and Power Platform. Referral codes come from affiliated investors, accelerators, incubators, angel networks, or universities in the Investor Network. This track started at $100,000 when the 2025 two-track system launched; amounts can vary by referral source and engagement.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup must be affiliated with a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network partner AND hold that partner's referral code. Company must otherwise meet MFS eligibility (for-profit, privately held, not Series C or later, < $350k lifetime free Azure credits). Universities now also count as referral sources, so a university-backed startup can use this path.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get a referral code from your affiliated investor/accelator/incubator/university. 2) Enter the code during the startups.microsoft.com application. 3) Upon approval, activate credits and redeem third-party benefits in the MFS portal (Azure Portal > Get started/benefits).
  - *Expiry:* Same rules as Startup credits: activate within 90 days of agreement; valid up to 2 years once activated. Third-party benefits (GitHub Enterprise etc.) have their own redemption windows.
  - *Gotchas:* Without a referral code you cap out at the self-serve/milestone path. Benefits can't be redeemed on accounts that already redeemed the same benefit or on existing enterprise accounts. Pegasus (dedicated architect/sales program) continued after the 2025 changes but is invitation-only and no longer referenced in current FAQ — do not plan around it.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/mfs-faqs
- **Azure Free Account (open to everyone, incl. students) — $200 trial + 12-month free services** (high) — Read today at azure.microsoft.com/free: $200 credit usable within 30 days; free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (e.g., 750 hrs each of B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 VMs, 32 GB PostgreSQL/MySQL storage, 720 vCore-hrs SQL Managed Instance, 100 GB Container Registry, 5 GB Cloud Shell/Storage); plus 65+ always-free services (e.g., 0.5M Speech chars/mo, 500 pages/mo Document Intelligence, 50 MB AI Search, 5,000 Vision transactions/mo). One free account per person. Spending protection: card won't be billed while covered; $1 temporary authorization possible.
  - *Eligibility:* New Azure customers, worldwide (card policy varies: HK and Brazil accept only credit cards). Students can claim this too, but if you're a student the Azure for Students offer below is better (no card, annual renewal). Not for people who already have a paid Azure account.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.microsoft.com/free with Microsoft account. 2) Add a phone number and a credit/debit card (non-prepaid). 3) $200 credit applies immediately; free monthly amounts auto-attach. 4) After 30 days you're notified to convert to pay-as-you-go or the account is disabled.
  - *Expiry:* $200 credit: 30 days (unused credit does not extend, even if you convert early). Free monthly service amounts: 12 months, then pay-as-you-go rates apply. Always-free tiers have no time limit but usage is capped.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial, not free: card required, and pay-as-you-go starts the moment the credit or the 12-month window ends. Free credit cannot be applied to Azure Marketplace offers, Spot VMs, or Hybrid Benefit. 6-month AI-services window previously advertised is no longer on the page — only the 12-month/always-free tiers are.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **Azure for Students — $100 credit for 12 months, no credit card (school email required)** (high) — Read today at azure.microsoft.com/free/students: $100 in Azure credit usable within 12 months, NO credit card required; free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months plus 65+ always-free services; full service catalog up to free amounts; 'renew your subscription annually and continue to get free access to Azure as long as you're a student'. A 'Starter' tier for younger users gives free service access only (no credit). The GitHub Student Developer Pack also routes 18+ students to this $100 offer (ages 13–17 get a reduced service set instead: App Service, Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL for apps, App Insights, DevOps).
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students; sign up with a school (.edu/institutional) email for verification. No card needed. One account per person. Age: the pack routes 13–17 to Starter; the $100 credit is for 18+. Third-party trackers (checked Oct 2026) list country restrictions — some regions only allow Starter; check the signup flow.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at azure.microsoft.com/free/students using your school email (or via the 'Microsoft Azure' offer in the GitHub Student Developer Pack after verification). 2) Confirm student status. 3) $100 credit activates immediately; free service amounts attach. 4) Renew annually while enrolled.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months from signup; annual renewal keeps the free services going while you remain a full-time student. 12-month free-service amounts reset with the renewal.
  - *Gotchas:* The $100 credit is the same offer the GitHub Pack grants — don't claim it twice (one Azure account per person; a second claim fails or voids the benefit). No card = no pay-as-you-go fallback: when the credit or free amounts run out, resources stop rather than bill you. Students who need more than free amounts should use the MFS self-serve $1,000 offer instead (which does need a card).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **GitHub Student Developer Pack — free Microsoft/educator stack (GitHub + M365 + Azure + tools)** (high) — Verified today on education.github.com/pack: while verified as a student you get free GitHub Pro, free GitHub Copilot 'Student' plan (unlimited completions + monthly GitHub AI credits, limited chat/agent on auto-selected models), 3-month Microsoft 365 Education (Word/Excel/PowerPoint/Teams/1TB OneDrive — duration on the current page says 'free or discounted', historically 3 months), $100 Azure credit (18+), Namecheap free .me domain (1 year) + free SSL, Heroku $13/month for 24 months, free annual-renewable JetBrains licenses, and a GitHub Foundations/Copilot certification voucher via Microsoft Learn (resumed Sept 2025). Enrollment requires being 13+, in a degree/diploma program; under-18s get a reduced offer set.
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled students: verify via school (.edu) email OR student ID + one proof-of-enrollment document (or a .edu email alone in some regions); 13+; some individual offers (Azure $100, Heroku) require 18+. No country limit stated for the pack itself.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at education.github.com/pack (or github.com/education) with school email or student ID. 2) Approval unlocks the pack page at github.com/features/benefits/students. 3) Redeem each partner offer individually (Azure, M365, Heroku, domain, etc.).
  - *Expiry:* Benefits last while you remain a verified student (GitHub Pro, M365, JetBrains renew annually/on graduation). Heroku credit: fixed 24 months. Domain/SSL: 1 year. Once you graduate, the pack stops renewing.
  - *Gotchas:* Partners churn: DigitalOcean ($200 credit) and Canva left the pack (DigitalOcean credits expire 2026-08-01), Bump.sh ended Dec 2025, Appwrite offer restructured to 6 months in Oct 2026. The Azure $100 in the pack IS the Azure for Students offer — one per person. 13–17-year-olds get no $100 credit, only a reduced service list.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack/
- **Microsoft 365 Education trial (30 days, schools) + free M365A license for institutions** (medium) — Microsoft offers schools a free 30-day Microsoft 365 Education trial (Word, Excel, PowerPoint, Teams, OneDrive, Class Notebook/whiteboard, Copilot in education apps, no credit card) claimable by an institution admin, and a free Microsoft 365 A1 (M365A) license for eligible K-12 schools and universities — through which most university students already get M365A at no personal cost via their school. Individual students at non-licensed institutions can use the free Office web apps/Word/Excel at no charge. (Official Microsoft page served localized/JS content and timed out, so the trial details are from the well-known program terms rather than a page read today — hence medium confidence.)
  - *Eligibility:* Trial: K-12 and higher-ed institutions (admin-managed). Free M365A: eligible institutions (K-12 schools and universities in supported countries); individual students get it only through an institutional license. No .edu email needed to sign the school up for the trial.
  - *How to claim:* 1) School: admin claims the 30-day trial from the M365 Education 'try it free' link on microsoft.com/en-us/education. 2) K-12: apply for the free M365A K-12 license via the Microsoft Education Store/partner flow. 3) Students: ask IT for the school's M365A license, or use free Office web apps with any email.
  - *Gotchas:* The trial is school-scoped, not individual: a 'free M365 for me' student offer does not exist standalone — students ride on institutional licenses or free web apps. Teams Education and Power Platform for Education trial similarly (30 days, institution-scoped).
  - *Source:* https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/education
- **Azure AI / Microsoft Foundry always-free tiers (available to students and startups alike)** (high) — From the Azure Free and Azure for Students pages read today: monthly always-free AI service amounts included with any free/12-month account: Azure Speech in Foundry Tools 0.5M neural characters/month; Document Intelligence 500 pages/month (S0); AI Search 50 MB storage (10k docs, 3 indexes); Vision in Foundry Tools 5,000 transactions/month per S1-S3 tier; Content Safety 5,000 text records or images/month; Azure DevOps free for 5 users; PostgreSQL/MySQL serverless free tiers. These stack on top of credit programs — useful for AI workloads while credits burn out.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Azure account; free monthly amounts are new-customer 12 months (VMs/databases) or perpetual (always-free services). Students with no card still get these via Azure for Students.
  - *How to claim:* No separate claim — free amounts auto-attach to eligible Azure services; monitor usage in Cost Management to stay under caps.
  - *Expiry:* Always-free: perpetual. 12-month amounts: until 12 months from account creation, then standard pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* Usage above the free amounts bills at normal rates (with no card, it just throttles/stops). Large AI inference (e.g., LLM tokens) is NOT in the always-free list — paid per token even in Foundry; budget accordingly.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free
- **2025–26 change:** Material 2025-2026 changes vs 'known so far': (1) The Founders Hub is now a single-entry 'Microsoft for Startups' program (apply at startups.microsoft.com). The no-investor baseline is the self-serve Azure startup offer — $1,000 for 90 days, then up to $4,000 more (180 days) after business verification (= up to $5,000); the older '~$200 starter + $4,000' framing now appears only as the no-code 'Build your startup' entry in the application flow. Investor-referred startups now START at ~$100,000 and grow to up to $150,000 (previously 'up to $150k' was the headline). (2) 2025-2026 rebrand: 'Azure OpenAI' and 'Azure AI Services' are now 'Azure AI Foundry' / 'Microsoft Foundry' (sub-brands Foundry Tools / Foundry Control Plane / Foundry IQ); the Azure free-services list was refreshed to these names. (3) LLM frontier: there is NO free OpenAI/LLM tier — access is paid/application-based and is only covered 'free' via the $200/$100/$1,000-$5,000 credits. (4) Several Azure AI services' older 'Free (F0) resource tiers' were replaced by the current 'always-free' monthly allowances on the free-services page (e.g., Content Safety 5,000/mo, Speech 0.5M chars/mo); exact F0-retirement dates were NOT re-verified this session because the session's web-search budget was exhausted mid-task. Everything else above is confirmed from the official Microsoft pages read today (overview/application/FAQ dated 2026-05 to 2026-10; pricing + free-services + Azure for Students pages current as of 2026-10-22). No major offer has been fully sunset; the main shifts are the rebrand and the consolidation of the startup credit tiers into one program.
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 program changes (verified via press + official MS pages): (1) July 1, 2025 — Microsoft for Startups restructure: the Founders Hub four-tier model (up to $150,000 in Azure credits, applied for with a validated business plan) was RETIRED, as were the 'Founders Hub' label, the Expert Network, and the Champion Network. It was replaced by a two-track system: a self-service track ($1,000 instantly for 90 days → up to $5,000 total for 180 days after business verification, for new Azure customers, credit card required for identity verification only) and an Investor Network track (VC/accelerator/university referral code; press-stated $100,000+ Azure credits, dedicated Microsoft POC, co-sell/Marketplace support). Pegasus (invitation-only dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect) was explicitly unchanged. Existing activated legacy credits stayed valid until their expiry, then auto-converted to pay-as-you-go; a Microsoft Q&A answer (July 2025) confirms non-investor applicants apply via the new 'Microsoft for Startups Credit Portal.' (2) June 2, 2026 — second update (official blog 'Microsoft for Startups: Apply and get credits to grow on Azure'): single entry point at startups.microsoft.com; startups 'start building immediately with Startup credits'; the up-to-$150,000 ceiling was RESTORED but now unlocked incrementally 'over time based on service adoption and sustained Azure usage and verified progress' rather than as a one-time grant; Investor Network partners unlock 'additional Startup credits, expanded support, and third-party benefits' earlier; current eligibility caps: not more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits, no Series C funding or later, private for-profit, HQ in an Azure region, no crypto mining; applications reviewed in ~3 business days (learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/overview, page updated 2026-08-05). (3) August 2025 — Azure Dev Tools for Teaching (successor to Microsoft Imagine) was DISCONTINUED: access disabled effective ~Aug 7-15, 2025; no new administrator signups after Aug 15, 2025; Microsoft provided NO direct replacement for educators — students' dev-tool benefits (incl. previously-bundled Visual Studio Pro) moved into the Azure for Students software-download list (now VS Community/Code-centric). Already-installed software keeps working. This is why the '12 months of Visual Studio Pro' line from the known-so-far notes no longer maps to any current Azure student offer (Pro/Enterprise IDE now comes via GitHub Student Developer Pack or Visual Studio Dev Essentials Premium). (4) The old 'Azure for College Students' offer ID (ms-azr-0170p) is still the legal offer terms page and matches today's $100/12-month/no-CC offer; no amount change in 2025-2026. (5) Not confirmed (could not re-verify today; pricing tables are JS-rendered and returned placeholders): exact Azure OpenAI free-trial token quotas and durations, and the Dev Essentials Premium $200/mo Azure-credit figure.
- **2025–26 change:** Verified structural changes as of 2026-10-22 (from pages fetched today): (1) The legacy 'Azure for Startups' program/pricing URLs are dead — azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/startup/ and /en-us/pricing/education now 404; the program is consolidated under 'Microsoft for Startups' at microsoft.com/en-us/startups with docs on learn.microsoft.com/startups (doc dates May–Oct 2026). - **2025–26 change:** TWO program sunsets in 2025-2026: (1) Effective ~June 27/July 1, 2025, the pre-2025 multi-tier Microsoft for Startups program — 'up to $150,000 in Azure credits' via L1 $1,000 / L2 $5,000 / L3 $25,000 / L4 $150,000 tiers, open to non-investor startups with a validated business plan — was retired and replaced by a two-track system: Investor Network track (~$100,000 start + additional awards, by investor referral) and a self-service track capped at $5,000 ($1,000 valid 90 days + $4,000 valid 180 days after business verification). Existing activated credits remained valid until expiry; no notice window, per The Register/TechRadar (July 1-2, 2025). The invitation-only Pegasus Program (dedicated Cloud Solutions Architect + Microsoft sales linkage) was reported unchanged in July 2025 but is not advertised anywhere in the 2026 MfS docs — likely quietly discontinued; confirm with MfS program support. (2) As of ~June 2, 2026, Microsoft replaced the two-track system with the current unified milestone model (see findings): anyone applies; $200 starter credits immediately (no investor code) or $1,000 (with investor code); up to $150,000 unlocked through business verification + sustained usage milestones (5/7/10 workloads, ~$3K/month). So the task's 'known so far' both figures are historical states: '$150,000' matches the pre-2025 program AND is the 2026 milestone ceiling; '~$100,000' matches the 2025 Investor-track start, not the current model. Marketing pages are lagging the docs: azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students still advertises the retired 2025 self-service track ('$1,000 instantly, up to $5,000, 90/180-day validity'), and the Founders Hub sign-up page still cites the legacy '$150,000 + $2,500 OpenAI credits' pitch; the official Learn docs (application page ms.date 2026-10-08) are the authoritative current state. Stale-URL note: https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/for-startups/ 404s today; the program now lives at microsoft.com/startups with docs under learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/. Note: the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted mid-task, so a few secondary items (MfS FAQ's exact URL, Azure for Students Starter per-service limits, Azure for Education page) were verified only indirectly and are marked medium/low confidence accordingly. - **2025–26 change:** No free offer for this front was sunset as of 2026-10-22, but several were materially changed in 2025-2026: (1) Microsoft for Startups was restructured into the tiered 'Founders Hub' model; the current headline is 'up to $150,000 in credits' (replacing the older 'up to $300,000 in total benefits' framing), with level-ups gated by an automated engagement score. (2) The live Cosmos DB 'free tier' is now the provisioned 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB allowance (confirmed on the current pricing page today); the previously documented separate 12-month serverless allowance (400,000 RU + 32 GB) does not appear on the current page, so plan against 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB. (3) The legacy Azure SQL Database 'free' provisioned F0 SKU has been retired/replaced by the perpetual serverless allowance (100,000 vCore-seconds/month + 32 GB storage) — if any docs still reference F0, they are stale. (4) Azure free account ($200 credit / 30 days + 12-month allowances) and Azure for Students ($100 credit / 12 months, no credit card) were unchanged in 2025-2026; note the student credit is $100, not $200 — $200 is the general new-customer trial. (5) Housekeeping: the dedicated SQL Database serverless pricing-details URL (azure.microsoft.com/en-us/pricing/details/sql-database/serverless/) now returns 404 as of today; the free allowances are instead confirmed on the /free/ and /free/students pages, and Azure has rebranded much of its AI surface to 'Microsoft Foundry' (product-name changes only, no free-allowance changes observed).

(2) Microsoft for Startups was restructured on July 1, 2025 (memo June 27, 2025): the old tiered program letting UNFUNDED startups reach $150,000 (L1 $1,000 / L2 $5,000 / L3 $25,000 / L4 $150,000) was pulled; replaced by a two-track system — Investor Network track (referral from affiliated VC/accelerator/university, credits starting at $100,000) and a self-service track capped at $5,000 ($1,000/90 days + $4,000/180 days post-verification). (6) Azure Dev Tools for Teaching stopped accepting new administrator signups after August 15, 2025 (existing admins retain access).
